<meta>This page continues the instructional and allegorical guidance for the alchemical process, focusing on the specific "Mercury Solution" and providing a rhyming summary of the entire Great Work.</meta>

* )( 0 )( *

NB. In all <term>cementations</term> <note>A process of heating a metal with powdered substances to alter its properties without melting it.</note> with Salt and Sulphur, there must be a gentle fire, so that it does not melt and only corrodes the substances; otherwise, it reduces the metallic bodies.

<term>Mercury Solution</term> <note>original: "☿. Solut." (Solutio Mercurii)</note>: one must learn to recognize this sign; it is not Mercury, nor is it the <term>sublimate</term> <note>A chemically purified substance, often mercuric chloride</note>, but rather a mineral being that does the most in this matter.

The entire universal work is presented in these rhymes, including the preliminary work and the subsequent work:

> Seek only the single One, the origin of all things,
> ->So that at the start, it does not fail you as it did me.<-
> The subsequent work is easy, but that which goes before is difficult;
> A wise man has much to do to swim through this sea.
> Once you have this thing, then separate from it
> Only the impure part; cast the pure into the grave.
> After it has lain in the grave for its allotted time,
> Then lead it out of the crypt; preserve this blessing.
> In it lies the treasure, upon it the field of stars;
> See that it returns again to our small world.
> Otherwise, the effort would be in vain; now begin to separate
> The Spirit from Soul and Body; in this, you may well avoid
> The great coal-fire, until Soul, Spirit, and Body
> Are separated according to the art; then give the man his wife.
> He will hold her fast, she will heartily embrace him,
> Each here stills their burning desire,
> And they are covered again with a black cloth,
> Under which surely lie the Whiteness and the Redness.
> Here proceed delicately, and "make haste slowly,"
> So that the white bride may bestow the pearl jewelry;
> For if you forfeit it, then the treasure is lost,
> Which the King himself has chosen as his hoard.
> Once you have the Pearl Crown, then the King will come,
> Clothed in purple, and, as I have heard,
> Requite your labor: Well for you, you have it good;
> Preserve this treasure as dear as your life and blood.
> Behold! thus is he blessed by GOD in this world,
> Who meets Him with pure love at all times;
> The pure Fear of God attains such a reward,
> The Fear of God alone crowns a son of virtue.

->( o )<-

#### Clear Explanation of the
## <term>Philosopher’s Stone</term> <note>The legendary substance capable of transmuting base metals into gold and achieving spiritual perfection.</note>,

--- Page 3 ---
<meta>Continuity with page 87: The text continues discussing alchemical procedures, moving from specific chemical instructions to a poetic summary of the "Universal" work.</meta>

->* )( 0 )( *<-

NB. In all <term>cementations</term> <note>a process where a metal is heated with dry powdered reagents to alter its properties without melting it</note> with salt and <term>Sulfur</term>, there must be a gentle fire so that the metal does not melt, but rather the substances merely corrode; otherwise, it reduces the <term>metallic bodies</term> <note>restores them to their base metal state prematurely</note>.

☿. Solution <note>The symbol ☿ represents Mercury. The text refers to "Solutio," the process of dissolving a solid into a liquid state.</note>: One must learn to recognize this sign; it is not common mercury, nor is it <term>sublimate</term> <note>mercury chloride</note>, but a mineral essence which performs the most important part of the work.

The entire "Universal" process is presented in these rhymes, including the preliminary work and the follow-up work:

> Seek only the single One, the origin of all things,
> So that in the beginning it does not fail for you as it did for me.
> The follow-up work is simple, but the preparation is difficult;
> A wise man has much to do to swim across this sea.
> Once you have this thing, then separate from it
> Only the impure part; cast the pure part into the grave <note>An allegory for putrefaction, the stage where the matter is broken down.</note>.
> After it has lain in the grave for its appointed time,
> Lead it forth from the crypt; preserve this blessing.
> In it lies the treasure, and upon it the field of stars;
> See that it returns again to our small world.
> Otherwise, the labor was in vain; now proceed to separate
> The Spirit from the Soul and Body; in this, you may well avoid
> The great coal fire, until Soul, Spirit, and Body
> Have been separated according to the Art; then give the man his wife <note>Refers to the "Chemical Wedding," the reunion of opposites.</note>.
> He will hold her fast, and she will heartily embrace him;
> Each here stills their burning desire.
> And they are covered again with a black cloth <note>Refers to the "Nigredo" or blackening stage.</note>,
> Beneath which surely hide the Whiteness and the Redness <note>The "Albedo" and "Rubedo," the final stages of the Great Work.</note>.
> Proceed cleanly here, and make haste slowly <note>A translation of the Latin "festina lente," a common alchemical maxim.</note>,
> So that the white bride may bestow the pearl jewelry.
> For if you squander it, the treasure is lost,
> Which the King himself has chosen for his own prize.
> Once you have the Pearl Crown, the King will come,
> Clothed in purple and—as I have heard—
> Repay your toil. Well for you, you are fortunate;
> Preserve this treasure as dearly as your own life and blood.
> Look! Thus is he blessed by God in this world,
> Who meets Him with pure love at all times.
> Pure fear of God attains such a reward;
> The fear of God alone crowns a son of virtue.

->The<-

->For a<-

## Clearer Explanation of the

# Philosophers' Stone,

<note>The "Philosophers' Stone" is the legendary substance in alchemy capable of turning base metals into gold and granting eternal life; here, it is treated as a spiritual and physical "growth."</note>

->How it stands and grows with its roots in the ex-<-
->ternal and dark world, with its blossom<-
->however in the Paradisiacal and Light-World, and<-
->with its ripe fruit in the Angelic<-
->and Heavenly World.<-

<note>This tripartite division of worlds—Dark, Light, and Heavenly—reflects the mystical cosmology of the 17th and 18th centuries, where the alchemical process mirrors the spiritual refinement of the soul.</note>

<image-desc>A decorative woodcut headpiece consisting of three horizontal bands of repeating floral rosettes and scrolled leaf patterns.</image-desc>

# Preface.

In my previous little treatise, which is called the Mineral Glue, the Double Serpent-Staff, the <term>Mercury of the Philosophers</term> <note>original: "Mercurius Philosophorum"; a symbolic, purified substance essential to alchemy, not common quicksilver</note>, the Long and Short Path to the Universal Tincture, I promised to publish another little treatise on the power and effect of the Tincture, especially its use in medicine. Since I used these formal terms toward the end of that little book: "Enough has been said about the growth of the three Principles, namely: 1. the outer world, 2. the inner and Light-World, and 3. the angelic and heavenly world"; however, I did not elaborate on these three worlds, nor did I explain myself at length regarding <term>multiplication</term> <note>the alchemical process of increasing the potency and quantity of the "Stone" or "Tincture"</note>. Because of this, the reader might wonder and want to know what was meant by it? Therefore, I have set aside that promised little treatise for now, and first, regarding the multiplication

<summary>In this preface, the author addresses his readers to clarify concepts from his previous book, specifically regarding the "Three Worlds" and the process of increasing alchemical power. He explains why he is delaying a promised medical text to focus on these theoretical foundations.</summary>
<keywords>Universal Tincture, Mercury of the Philosophers, three principles, multiplication, alchemical medicine, preface</keywords>

--- Page 7 ---
<meta>This page continues the author’s explanation of the "multiplication" of the alchemical growth. The previous page ended with the author stating he would first explain the multiplication of the "growth of the three Principles."</meta>

-> ❁ ):( o ):( ❁ <-

of this growth of the 3 Principles, according to all 3 worlds, I wish to explain myself better; namely, which 3 worlds I understand philosophically <note>original: "Philosophice"</note> in our Work, because it possesses 3 kingdoms just as the great world <note>the macrocosm or the universe at large</note> does. 

Namely: 1. the mineral: because it has its beginning and origin from this. 2. the vegetable, because it grows; for all 7 metals grow out of it, and from such metals the shining planets—like bright sparkling stars—the most pure gemstones and <term>tinctures</term> <note>highly potent alchemical substances used for transmutation or medicine</note>. 3. the animal, because it has nothing but living spirits, such as earth-spirits, water-spirits, air-spirits, and fire-spirits <note>a reference to the four elemental beings: gnomes, undines, sylphs, and salamanders</note>. 

But someone might ask me: why then did I call the heavens and Paradise a world? To this the answer serves: that there is a great difference between our heavenly and paradisiacal world and the Divine Heaven and Paradise; ours is only a mirror in which we glimpse the latter, knowing well that God created not just one throne of the holy angels, but many. Also, that heaven is not a world as it now stands with the innumerable multitude of stars, where each has its own particular heaven in which it floats—just like the yolk in the egg (which carries the yolk)—and indeed with such an immeasurable size that our world is hardly as large against—

<meta>catchword: against</meta>

<summary>The author explains how the alchemical "Work" mirrors the three kingdoms of nature (mineral, vegetable, and animal) and clarifies that the alchemist's "heaven" is a terrestrial mirror of the Divine realm. He uses the metaphor of a yolk in an egg to describe the relationship between stars and their respective celestial spheres.</summary>
<keywords>three principles, three kingdoms, mineral, vegetable, animal, elemental spirits, microcosm, macrocosm, celestial spheres, cosmology</keywords>

against it as a ball that one can hold in the hand. Whether such great bodies <note>referring to the planets and stars mentioned on the previous page</note> are also inhabited by angels or other spirits (since indeed there is no empty place in all of nature <note>The author refers to the classical "horror vacui" theory, the belief that nature contains no true vacuum and is filled with matter or spirit</note>) or whether they are perhaps occupied by other creatures? This we do not know; it is known to God alone. 

But of the different heavens, we know from Holy Scripture: As in the **1st Book of Kings, chapter 8, verse 27**, it stands: *Behold, the heaven and the heaven of all heavens cannot contain thee.* **Job, chapter 15, verse 15**: *The heavens are not clean in His sight.* **Psalm 19, verse 1**: *The heavens declare the glory of God.* **Psalm 89, verse 6**: *The heavens shall, O Lord, praise Thy wonders.* **Job, chapter 41, verse 2**: *Whatever is under all the heavens is mine.* **Psalm 102, verse 26**: *The heavens are the work of Thy hands.* **Psalm 136, verse 5**: *To Him who made the heavens in an orderly way.* **Psalm 148, verse 1**: *Praise the Lord from the heavens;* **verse 4**: *Praise Him, ye heavens everywhere.* **The 5th Book of Moses <note>Deuteronomy</note>, chapter 2, verse 25**: *All peoples under the whole heaven shall tremble when they hear of thee.* **2 Corinthians, chapter 12, verse 2**: *Paul was caught up into the third heaven.* And in more places of the Holy Scripture. 

But of our heaven, under which we live, as far as our eyes reach, we have this <term>science</term> <note>original: "Wissenschafft"; here meaning systematic knowledge or revealed wisdom rather than modern experimental science</note> from the Holy Scripture, from the first chapter—

-> ❂ ):( (0) ):( ❂ <-

from the first book of Moses: that it was created from the best, purest, and brightest part of the waters and was called the firmament or heaven, and from the purest part of light and fire, the stars. Indeed, Moses gave us no light beforehand <note>meaning Moses did not describe the state of existence prior to the physical creation</note>, but wrote only of the creation of this world upon which we live. 

But the earth upon which we now walk, and the height of the heaven as far as our eyes reach the sky and stars, was once the throne, seat, and heaven of **Lucifer**. In that place, there were no stars, neither sun nor moon; rather, this light was mixed with the clear, pure waters of the purest earth—all a pure, beautiful throne and heaven, brightly shining and gleaming, in which Lucifer was created so that he should rule this heaven or throne eternally. 

This was the most beautiful princely and throne-angel, created from the light of the **Son of God** and thereby transfigured <term>transfigured</term> <note>original: "verklähret"; meaning to be imbued with divine radiance or celestial glory</note>. But when he beheld this light within himself, he marveled at his own beauty and began to become haughty. He wished to be even prouder, to make himself even more beautiful, and to transfigure himself with even greater light, so that he might be equal to the Son of God, or even above him. Therefore, he ignited his own light in all his <term>source-spirits</term> <note>original: "Quel-Geistern"; a term central to Behmenist mysticism, referring to the fundamental spiritual qualities or "fountains" of energy that constitute a being's essence</note>, even though his birth...

and was God's creation. If he was to have been further transfigured and glorified, it would have had to happen through GOD, for GOD alone is the Creator. Immediately, the divine light was extinguished in them, and his whole legion of angels with their heaven caught fire; for the heaven burned both in its seven <term>source-spirits</term> <note>original: "Quel-Geiſtern"; Jacob Böhme’s term for the fundamental spiritual forces that "well up" to create all reality</note> as well as the angels themselves. In these ignitions, everything was damaged, and the entire heaven was reduced to a <term>confused chaos</term> <note>original: "Confuſum Chaos"; a Latin term for the formless state of matter before the Creation described in Genesis</note>. 

But because God’s omnipotence and will had to prevail, God nevertheless separated from this confused chaos first the light water from the dark; He separated the light from the darkness; He separated the ensouled water from the earth out of the midst of the water, from which He made the firmament of the heavens. He separated the water under the firmament from the water above the firmament, which is the upper heaven, in which the stars float like a yolk in an egg. 

Out of the ignited light and fire, God created the stars, and out of the coarse part, the earth upon which we walk. This now—and the whole heaven as far as we can see, in which the stars run—is the entire kingdom of Lucifer. He governed it in such a way that it must now all run about in <term>surging anguish</term> <note>original: "quellende Angſt"; a technical term in this mysticism describing the restless, churning quality of nature in its fallen or material state</note>, in evil...

In this ignition and welling birth of anguish it shall remain until the end of the world, for all creatures are in anguish and long for their liberation; they wish to be free from the vanity to which they are subjected, and from the bonds of division into evil and good, into which they fell through the ignition of **Lucifer**. They would gladly reappear with all their powers as one spirit in the former pure, clear heaven and <term>splendor</term> <note>original: "Splendor"; Latin for brilliance or radiance</note> before **God**, and wish to bear heavenly, permanent fruits instead of the current fleeting and deadly ones. 

So also will **God**, the Creator of heaven and earth, have mercy, and let the anguish and sighing of nature and the creature go to His heart. When the number of the fallen angels is fulfilled, and the ungodly have also ignited the <term>great turmoil</term> <note>original: "Turbam magnam"; Latin for "the great crowd" or "great disturbance," referring to the chaotic state of the wicked and the elemental world before judgment</note> through their evil deeds, then **God** will pour out the seven bowls of the wrath of **God**, which are the final plagues, upon the earth and the elements. He will burn them in their own fire and return the heavenly waters to the pure, transfigured earth, to reunite with its own stars, lights, and fire, and return to the former heaven and clear

<meta>This page continues the sentence from page 10, where the author was explaining his intention for writing: he has "therefore first sought" to lead the reader toward a spiritual understanding of God and the self.</meta>

<page-num>11</page-num>

<header>) : ( o ) : (</header>

...therefore first sought to lead [him] into **GOD**, and shown him how gloriously he was created, how deeply he fell, and how he—after the dying off of his animal flesh and blood, after the killing of the <term>old Adam</term> <note>A biblical concept from the letters of St. Paul, referring to the fallen, sinful nature of humanity that must be "put off" to achieve spiritual rebirth.</note>, might find the <term>new man</term> <note>The spiritually reborn self, restored to the divine image.</note> again, and be able to unite himself with **GOD** even here [on earth], so that he lives here in the Spirit of Christ and is a true child of **God**. 

Thus, the Holy Spirit—the Spirit of wisdom and understanding, of counsel and of might—wishes to take us into His school and teach us everything. This is the true <term>high school</term> <note>original: "hohe Schule"; referring not to a modern secondary school, but to an academy of higher divine learning or a spiritual university.</note>, far above all the wisdom of the Egyptians, Chaldeans, and Persians. We see this in Moses, Joseph, David, Solomon, and Daniel; they all possessed supernatural wisdom. For those who live in the "new man," **GOD** enlightens with His Holy Spirit; and the <term>new birth</term> <note>The state of being "born again" spiritually.</note> is born of **GOD**, whose heaven and <term>inclination</term> <note>original: "Inclination"; in the mystical traditions of this period, this refers to the inner spiritual direction or the "magnetic" pull of the soul toward God.</note> is **GOD** Himself, and the holy angels are their stars. These have nothing to do with the natural, starry heavens; they rule over them, and their works have a higher origin from **GOD**. 

The holy apostles and prophets were also such men, filled with the Holy Spirit and clothed with light and power from on high, which is far more than the <term>spirit of the great world</term> <note>original: "Geist der grossen Welt"; referring to the *Spiritus Mundi*, the vitalizing spirit or astral influence of the macrocosm/universe.</note>, which is but an influx of the stars. They were taught in the school of the Holy Spirit, such...

## Contents of the First Treatise.

The first treatise concerns the Universal <note>The "Universal" refers to the "Universal Medicine" or "Philosopher's Stone," a substance believed to be able to perfect both the human soul and physical matter.</note> according to the growth of the three principles, and how it corresponds to all three worlds: the outer and dark world, the light and paradisiacal world, and the angelic and heavenly world. Within this, his entire creation, according to all three worlds, is touched upon universally in summary. It is described in its coarse, impure, and dark nature—what forms, smells, and tastes exist in the outer world, including all seven source-spirits <note>original: "Quell-Geister"; a term famously used by the mystic Jacob Böhme to describe the seven fundamental qualities or forces that drive all of existence.</note> in their harsh, bitter, sour, and fierce quality contained therein. From this foul nature, it must be separated and placed into the light, into <term>the</term> <note>original "dz," a common contemporary abbreviation for "das" (the/that)</note> shining, healthy water of life. There, all seven source-spirits become pure, white, sweet, bright, and shining; finally, they all emerge as one, becoming tangible and attaining a perfect nature, which is called the construction of Paradise. Also described is how this paradisiacal body is transferred into the angelic and heavenly world, where these seven forms of nature—through the pure fire-spirits, which are the seven source-spirits of the angelic world—transform these paradisiacal bodies into bright-shining stars and gemstones. Thus, our seven metals become the planetary stars that travel upon the high axes and are transformed into pure heavenly spirits through the air and heavens of the angelic world, which is called the triple magic wheel, and the three times seven

## The First Treatise.

**H**is land lies in the blessing of the **Lord**; there are noble fruits from heaven, from the dew, and from the deep that lies beneath. This is the threefold blessing in the books of Moses. First, Isaac blessed his son Jacob with it; also Jacob blessed Joseph and gave him this blessing; also Moses, before his death, blessed the tribe of Joseph with it again.

...must follow, maintaining this very same process and walking this path, for the word *Fiat* <note>Latin: "Let there be"; the creative command of God</note> is still to this day *in esse* <note>Latin: "in being" or "in existence"</note>, which Prince Lucifer well understood. He wished to create himself as a god, equal to the Son of God, because he beheld his own light within himself; thus he wished to transfigure himself even further with it. This, however, could not be; there must remain only one GOD and he shall have no secondary gods beside Him. So GOD took the radiance—the light of the Son, as His heart and light—away from Lucifer, and then he was a devil. His seven source-spirits <note>original: "Quel-Geister"; the seven fundamental active forces or "fountains" of being in Boehmian theosophy</note>, which he had ignited within himself as well as in his entire throne-heaven in nature, burned him into a black, withered coal and a hellish extinguished brand <note>original: "Löschbrand"; refers to a piece of wood that has been charred and then put out, signifying a soul devoid of divine light</note>, for the sweet light-water of the Son of God was withdrawn from him. 

But because God's holy counsel decreed that this region—from which Lucifer was thrust and which he had ignited with fierce source-spirits—should thereafter be preserved and eternally filled and inhabited by the new creatures of mankind, God created the current world upon which we live, with the heaven and seven planets above us, out of this desolate lump that had been reduced by that conflagration into such a hard, fierce, dark, and gloomy essence. The Earth was previously the beautifully shining throne of Lucifer, which GOD will also one day renew. He says: "Behold, I make all things new; I will create a new heaven and a new earth, in which righteousness dwells." He also said to Abraham: "Lift up your eyes, and see—"

...has as an answer, that this undertaking does not occur for the reason that man wishes to become equal to **GOD** thereby; rather, he undertakes this work out of love for **GOD**. In it, he seeks the wonders of God, His power and omnipotence, which He has placed into nature, His creation, which he wishes to laud and praise for the glory of God. From this, as in a mirror, he might recognize himself, the creature, and also nature: how everything comes from **GOD**, and how He has imprinted and sunken Himself into everything and granted His Divine power to them. This is especially true in this *Matter* <term>Materia: in this context, the "first matter" or the physical substance used in alchemical work to reveal divine signatures</term>, which He intended to serve for the good and benefit of fallen man—who is plagued by much sickness and poverty—and which He has shown and pointed out to holy men.

Because the holy Patriarchs <term>Erzväter: the biblical fathers, such as Abraham or Hermes Trismegistus in the Hermetic tradition, seen here as the original keepers of divine secrets</term> saw that most people lived in the kingdom of the devil and hell, as if in the realm of God’s wrath, they did not deem such people worthy of the mystery. Yet, they wished to pass it on to the future believing children of God so that this science <note>original: "Wissenschafft"; here referring to "Scientia," a sacred and systematic knowledge of natural and divine laws</note> should not perish. Therefore, they described it in hieroglyphic figures and veiled writings, so that only the Elect might practice this Divine study, while other wild, beast-like men should be excluded from it.

Thus, it is neither a sin nor against **GOD** to practice this, using it for **GOD’S** honor and for the benefit of one's neighbor.

...must depart from it through death, and they must also endure it, just like the great world <note>The "great world" refers to the Macrocosm or the universe, as opposed to the "little world" or Microcosm of the human being.</note>, since the earth must bear thorns and thistles. The text says in the first book of Moses in the first chapter: In the beginning **GOD** created heaven and earth, and the earth was formless and empty, and it was dark upon the depths, and the Spirit of God moved upon the water, and **GOD** spoke: Let there be light, and there was light; then **GOD** divided the light from the darkness, and called the light Day, and the darkness Night; then from evening and morning the first day was made. This workday of **GOD** is also our first workday, our beginning, where the earth is still mixed with the water, and all elements are together in a formless lump; but the spirit of this world hovered upon the water and ripened it, driving the dark, cloudy, impure, and dreg-like matter to the bottom.

From this work it is now to be seen that in the creation, the word <term>Ruach</term> <note>Hebrew for "spirit," "wind," or "breath"</note> does not mean the [Holy] Spirit that hovered upon the water, but rather the **Spirit of Nature**. This is the spirit of the great world, acting according to all seven <term>Source-Spirits</term> <note>original: "Quel-Geistern"; a concept from theosophy (likely Jacob Böhme) referring to the seven fundamental qualities or forces that drive all existence</note> which are hidden within this **confused chaos** and which took their beginning from the seven Source-Spirits of God. This was intended to be the world's own <term>Separator</term> <note>A term for a force that divides and distinguishes different substances or qualities from a mixture</note> and divider; it was created by **GOD** for that purpose, and He gave it the power so that the work could be done through it, because this **World-Spirit** [acts] only for the fertility of the world...

...should consist. And why should the Holy Spirit of God hover upon this water? He did not need to enlighten or sanctify it, because no **Divine Birth** was to come from it—as was the case in the body of the **Virgin Mary**, whom He overshadowed—but rather it was merely the outgrowth of this great world, corrupted by **Prince Lucifer**, which He wished to create anew from that corruption, out of the desolate lump. This [world] is also to stand for seven days, that is, 7,000 years, within its seven **Source-Spirits** <note>original: "Quel-Geistern"; the fundamental active forces of nature in theosophy</note>, according to the number of the **seven planets**. And when these have finished their work through six great days of toil and labor, the seventh day shall be their **Sabbath**, and they shall stand in the seventh Source-Spirit in **Divine Contemplation** <note>original: "Beschaulichkeit"; a state of peaceful, spiritual looking or meditation</note>. 

Oh, who shall be alive when **GOD** does this? It is the end of Nature, at which point the eternal Kingdom begins. **GOD** said: Let there be light; then **GOD** divided the light from the darkness, and called the light Day, and the darkness Night. When the **confused chaos** <note>original: "confusum Chaos"; the primeval, unorganized state of matter</note> has been ripened by His Spirit as the true **Separator** <note>A term for the divine or alchemical power that divides mixed elements into their pure forms</note> so that the light can be divided from this dark lump, then the light spirit is called Day, and the remaining dreg-like <note>original: "hefenhafftige"; meaning full of sediment or impurities, like the dregs of wine</note> matter is the Night; this is the first **Day’s Work**, and from evening and morning the first day is made. 

At this time, neither sun nor moon had yet been created, neither day nor night; here the bright and pure spirit is called Day, and the dark earth is called Night, and one day’s work is completed. This is the first day for the **Follower** <note>original: "Nacharbeiter"; literally "after-worker," referring to the alchemist or seeker who imitates God's creative process in their own spiritual or chemical labor</note>, but as yet there is no separation...

<meta>This page continues the commentary on the Creation narrative, moving from the Second Day (the firmament) to the Third Day, where the physical earth is established and biological life begins through the interaction of the elements.</meta>

<page-num>22</page-num>

-> ❀  ):( O :(  ❀ <-

...the water under the heavens to specific places, so that the dry land may be seen; and He calls the dry land Earth, and the gathering of the waters He calls Sea. And **GOD** spoke: Let the earth bring forth grass, and herbs that seed themselves, and fruitful trees, that each according to its kind bears fruit, and has its own seed within itself upon the earth; then from evening and morning came the third day. Here, the text now speaks of the complete separation of the <term>Elements</term> <note>The four basic building blocks of the universe: Earth, Water, Air, and Fire</note>, which is called a dispersal of the elements. Because the earth was composed out of the water, it is now separated from it, yet also moistened by it once more—specifically by the sea-water, which draws itself upward into the clouds and comes down again as rain. Through this, the earth becomes fruitful, so that everything grows from her, and each thing has its seed within itself, by which it multiplies. Although the <term>Elements</term> appear to be purely separated—both in the great world <note>the macrocosm or universe</note> and in our own small world <note>the microcosm or human being</note>—nevertheless, the earth still contains all elements within itself. The water is also full of air, fire, and flavorful fruits; the air also contains water, fire, and earth; the fire also contains earth, water, and air. It must indeed be so, for the "simple" <note>pure or unmixed</note> elements would be without power and a dead being. This is all to be found and seen in the <term>Refining Work</term> <note>original: "Nacharbeit"; in alchemy, this refers to the practical laboratory stages that follow the initial separation of raw matter</note>, when the <term>Artist</term> <note>original: "Künstler"; a traditional term for the alchemist or practitioner of the Great Work</note> has separated the elements and made the earth fruitful through the moistening of its own and remaining water; thus, from evening and morning, the third day is come, the third work completed.

takes [possession], making himself Lord and Ruler, so that soon the bottom-most becomes the top-most, and the top-most becomes the bottom-most <note>A reference to the Hermetic principle "As above, so below," implying a total transformation or inversion of the substance</note>; but before this, Adam and Eve must be in Paradise, in pleasure and joy; however, they are driven by the Cherub into the outer corrosive <note>original: "corrosivische"; in alchemy, this refers to the harsh, acidic, or destructive state of base matter after it has "fallen" from its pure state</note> world, where there is nothing but misery and wretchedness, sorrow and pain. Just as **GOD** did not let the first created world completely dissolve or vanish in the water, so the practitioner <term>Nacharbeiter</term> <note>literally "after-worker"; one who follows the steps of the Great Architect/God</note> must carefully consider that he does not let the earth dry out too much (which would be of no use), nor should he let it completely dissolve or melt through moistening and rain, but rather it should remain fertile. Thus, when the waters of the Deluge have dried up, the sign of grace—the rainbow—can appear. I will explain this work even better and more extensively once its many names have first been interpreted, through which the reader learns to recognize it.

as our **Confused Chaos** <note>original: "Confusum Chaos"; the primordial, unrefined state of matter from which the world and the philosopher's stone are created</note> from God the Creator, which Nature has given to us; the **raw matter** <note>original: "Materia cruda"</note>, which is called by so many names, such as:

1. **Azoth** <note>a universal alchemical solvent, often identified with the "Mercury of the Philosophers"</note>, because from it the true **Root-Vinegar** <note>original: "Wurzel-Essig"; also known as Acetum Radicalis, a powerful solvent used to dissolve metals</note> is made.

2. **Saturn** [♄]. The begetting-father of all gods, from whom the golden **Genealogy** of the gods descends, who devours all his children <note>In both mythology and alchemy, Saturn (representing lead) is the "Old Man" who must be dissolved or "consumed" to release the inner spirit of the metals</note>.

3. **Electrum**, because it contains all 7 metals within itself, and the true **artificial Electrum** <note>a specialized alloy believed to have magical or medicinal properties</note> is to be made from it; however, on the outside it is coated with a white **Mercury** [☿] so that it has a different color, so that its inner beauties are not seen and it remains hidden from so many pursuers.

4. **Magnesia**, because due to its hot and dry nature it attracts all **acids** <note>original: "acida"</note> to itself, and whatever it attracts is its prisoner—especially the **Spirit of the World** <note>original: "Spiritum Mundi"</note>, the **aerial nitre** <note>original: "nitrum aëreum"; a vital, life-giving substance thought to be extracted from the air by alchemical salts</note>.

5. Our **Lodestone** <note>original: "Magnetstein"</note>, because its inner central pole or <term>Salt</term> <note>In the alchemical trinity of Salt, Sulfur, and Mercury, Salt represents the physical body, stability, and the principle of "fixity"</note> is our small world <note>the microcosm</note>, and it draws all its purified parts into itself like a magnet, retaining them in the form of a dry and transfigured body; it coagulates into a firm stone when the purified elements are joined together again, which thereafter can nevermore be separated.

6. **Hermetic Ore** <note>original: "Aes Hermetis"</note>, because the philosophical metals are hidden here; **Hermes** also means the serpent, and this is the metallic poisonous serpent <note>The serpent often represents the volatile, "venomous" initial state of the matter that must be tamed</note>.

7. **Prime Matter** <note>original: "Prima Materia"</note> of the Stone, because it is the first substance or material for it.

10. The art consists in this: to unite these two, which is the greatest effort in the whole work; it is the "bride around whom one dances"—specifically, how one should unite the **red servant** with the **white woman**. Whoever can do this has won.

11. **The Stone or Rock** that gives oil, wine, blood, and milk; the **four elements** <note>original: "4. Elementa"</note> and the many **solvents** <note>original: "Menstrua"; in alchemy, a liquid used to dissolve or extract essence</note>; the land in which milk and honey flow.

13. **A Stone**, because it is a hard, dry, and earthly being; afterwards it is our <term>Antimony [⊕]</term> stone, then the glass-like **Azoth** <note>a universal solvent</note> or **Crystal-Stone** of <term>Mercury [☿]</term>. Finally, it becomes the **white Tincture**, the **Water-Stone of the Sages**, and at last it becomes the **Ruby** and **Carbuncle-Stone**.

14. **Golden Tree** and its root; it has no <term>Sun [☉]</term> <note>gold</note> nor <term>Moon [☽]</term> <note>silver</note>, but it carries much volatile <term>Moon [☽]</term> sulfur and the blood-red purple sulfur of gold; and it possesses the unique <term>Mercury [☿]</term> within itself, which allows itself to be readily **refined** <note>original: "gradiren"; to increase the purity or "grade" of a metal</note> by its own sulfur. This <term>Mercury [☿]</term> is the tree that bears the blossoms of the Suns and Moons, which also ripen upon it and multiply many thousand-fold.

15. **First Matter** <note>original: "Prima materia"</note> of all metals; firstly, because all seven philosophical metals are begotten from it. But when the **elements** <note>original: "Elementa"</note> are separated and the first composition is undertaken, then it is the **first matter** <note>original: "prima materia"</note> of all metals, because here the planets must run through the entire **Zodiac** <note>original: "Zodiacum"</note> until the Sun enters its own house in <term>Leo [♌]</term>, and the **Lion [♌]** has devoured the Sun, because he [takes] his beginning—

...has the origin and progress of this Matter.

16. **Pyrites**, **Flint**, because the stone of fire <note>original: "ignis"</note> is to be made from it: the fiery **Carbuncle**, which shines in the dark; the fiery, gleaming **Urim and Thummim** <note>Hebrew: sacred stones used by the biblical High Priest for divination; here representing the inner light of the stone</note>; the coagulated and hardened fire.

17. **Lemnian Earth** <note>original: "Terra lemnia"; a medicinal red clay traditionally from the island of Lemnos</note>, **red clay**, because from this red earth come the red <term>Sulfur [🜍]</term>, the **red man**, and the **white woman**. But because there are many other such names, and these many names do not help the matter, one rightly lets them go and strives only to learn his work, as **purification** must come first, followed by **separation** and **composition**. 

There is only one God in heaven, and only one **Matter** <note>original: "Materia"</note> in the world from which this is to be made, which contains: 1. the **Matter**, 2. the two **sulfurs**, man and woman, red and white <term>Sulfur [🜍]</term>, 3. the three **Principles** <note>original: "3. Principia"; typically Salt, Sulfur, and Mercury</note>, 4. the four **Elements** <note>original: "4. Elementa"</note>. Once all these are purified, separated, and sought out together again, then the work is done; but without the **unlocked gates of the elements**, nothing can be achieved. 

Instead of the earth upon which we walk, our earth—our **Laton** <note>an alchemical term for the crude or "unclean" metallic body at the start of the work</note>—must be taken, which is of two kinds: 1. the **red clay**, loam, or earth; the other is the **Virgin Salt** called **Hyle** <note>Greek for "matter" or "substance"</note>, and both come from a single source. No **composition** is made with the red Laton because it must be separated from it again—and it must be separated because fire and water cannot be united. The first **composition** occurs with the pure salt or the pure earth and water. It is also not common air or common water, for if...

<summary>The author concludes the list of allegorical names for the First Matter, emphasizing that it contains all chemical principles and elements. He specifies that the "Earth" used in the work is a special substance called Laton or Hyle, rather than literal soil.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the discussion of the First Matter, transitioning from its names to its properties and its relationship to the three kingdoms of nature (Mineral, Vegetable, and Animal). It explains why the "metallic seed" is the ideal starting point for the Philosopher's Stone.</meta>

<page-num>29</page-num>

<header>->❁ ):( 0 ):( ❁<-</header>

Our **metallic seed** is not far removed <note>meaning it is already close to its perfected state</note>; otherwise, a human’s life would be too short to create from it a **tincturing stone** <note>the Philosopher's Stone, capable of "tincturing" or changing the nature of metals</note> that would be fixed and incombustible in fire, for our <term>Matter [Materia]</term> must be incombustible. The Stone is made **from metals, through metals, and with metals**, though the <term>Matter [Materia]</term> is not strictly a metal itself, but rather the **flower of metals**; it is also not strictly a mineral, though most closely related to minerals. It is **vegetable** <note>In alchemy, "vegetable" refers to the power of growth and reproduction, not just plants</note> because it grows and is endowed with a **living soul**; from it is also prepared the vegetable life-giving water, which can be applied to all **three kingdoms** <note>The three kingdoms of nature: Mineral, Vegetable, and Animal</note>.

In the **mineral kingdom**, it is very useful because it transforms such bodies into **Gold [☉]** and **Silver [☽]**. In the **vegetable kingdom**, it is even more useful, making all old trees fertile; one can perform many thousands of wonders in this kingdom with it, because all vegetable **essences** can be separated in a short time by this **fire**. In the **animal kingdom**, it is most magnificent of all. What is more important than health? This **balsam** heals from both within and without. If God had not ordained death because of sin, humans would be rendered immortal through it. 

These pure **elements** <note>original: "Elementa"</note> now seem indeed pure and separated, yet it is impossible to separate them entirely; the **earth** still possesses a **sulfurous fatness**, and the **Mercury [☿]** or water even more so. The **air** also carries **Mercury [☿]** or water with it, and the **fire** contains much that is earthly, like soil—all of which can be seen in the **composition** <note>the final combined state of the alchemical ingredients</note>.

<meta>catchword: This</meta>

<meta>This page transitions from the general properties of the "metallic seed" to the specific process of purifying the Matter [Materia]. <page-num>30</page-num>

<header>🌼 ):( (o) ):( 🌼</header>

This **Matter [Materia]** must be very well purified; its coarse earthly body is of no use for this purpose. The Work is performed only with the pure parts, because the whole body <note>original: *Corpus*</note> is not used, but only the blood and soul of the "old man." When he is led into his **bath of purification** so that his external leprosy <note>In alchemy, "leprosy" refers to the impurities, oxides, or "crust" on the surface of base metals</note> is washed away, only then is he our **Adam**, who is to multiply the world. This Adam is pregnant with his wife, his **Eve**, who is separated from him; thus Adam remains behind as our **red clay**, **Laton** <note>An alchemical term for the "base" metal, often associated with the color change from black to red</note> or loam, from which Laton the **Paradise-Water** is made. This is the stream that springs from Paradise and divides into four rivers:

The first, **Pishon** [Pison], is the **Ganges** in India, which carries much gold, and the gold of that land is precious; it also contains the gemstone onyx and **bdellium** <note>original: *Bedellion*, a fragrant resin mentioned in the Bible alongside gold and onyx</note>. The second, **Gihon**, flows around the land of the Ethiopians <note>original: *Mohrenland*, literally "land of the Moors," the biblical Cush</note>; it is the **Nile** [Nylus] in Egypt, where the great serpent, the **crocodile**, lives. The third is named **Hiddekel**, which is the **Tigris** in Assyria. The fourth is the **Phrath**, or **Euphrates** in Syria. These two rivers are in one land and belong together.

The other is the poisonous serpent, the **tincturing venom** <note>original: *venenum tingens*</note>, our **cloacal oil** <note>meaning a waste-like or foul-smelling starting substance, often associated with the "filth" from which the Stone is born</note>. The first is our golden <term>Antimony [♁]</term>, through which the **Stone** becomes **Gold [☉]**, which is transparent and shining like a gemstone—**Gold [☉]** in the form of glass.

When this woman is now separated from the man—**Eve taken from Adam**—she must be joined to him again in marriage, so that she may conceive by him and become pregnant. This is our second **Confused Chaos** <note>original: *Confusum Chaos*, the undifferentiated state of matter recreated by the alchemist through "Art"</note>, made through art, within which the four elements are hidden and from which the many **menstrua** <note>the solvents or life-giving "blood" of the metals used to dissolve and nourish the work</note> emerge—

He bears the crown and scepter and is also our **Sun and Moon**; yet he must suffer and die through the serpent’s sting. Through this death, he is first truly transfigured and becomes the **White Stone**, which is hereafter transferred into the heavenly and angelic world, so that he can never die again but is eternally living. This **Matter** <note>original: *Materia*</note> is of a very contrary nature when the elements are separated, for they all host enmity toward one another and are adverse, after the fashion of the outer dark world. In the **Multiplication** <note>The stage in the Great Work where the power and quantity of the Philosopher's Stone is increased.</note>, however, the Sun must be united with the Sun, which once again is not the common sun or <term>Gold</term> <note>Symbol: ☉</note>, but our Sun is the decocted, fixed <note>original: *figirte*; rendered stable and resistant to fire.</note> matter of the Stone, which has cooked itself into gold through its own <term>Mercury</term> <note>Symbol: ☿</note>. Such gold, being saturated by its own flesh and blood, is a pure fiery, hot, and red **Tincture** <note>A substance capable of "tinging" or transforming base metals into gold.</note>; it is far higher than common gold, and no higher, better, or more perfect gold is to be found in all of nature.

This gold or solar-radiance is bestowed with the name of the **Crowned Red <term>Lion</term>** <note>Symbol: ♌︎; a high-level representation of the fixed, solar principle.</note>, and it is the red, bloody Stone of the first order, which we marry to our other sun, the <term>Solar Mercury</term> <note>Symbol: ☿ solare</note>—our red flying <term>Lion</term> <note>Symbol: ♌︎; the "flying" or volatile version of the principle that must be united with the fixed version.</note>.

We also have two Moons in the Multiplication for the **White Tincture**. The first Moon is the decocted, dry, fixed substance of our Stone of the first order, which is called the **White Crowned Queen**. If she is now to be multiplied, she must be **resolved** <note>original: *resolviret*; dissolved or returned to a liquid state.</note> again with the **Starry Eagle** <note>Often refers to the volatile spirit or the "mercury of the philosophers" used to dissolve the fixed matter.</note>, with the imperishable **living water**, which consists of body, soul, and spirit, so that she bathes in her blooming blood and sweat—both of which can be done infinitely <note>original: *in Infinitum*</note>. When our **Adam** is pregnant with his **Eva**, then our Sun and our

The Moon in dark shadow and a thick curtain is murky, dark, and gloomy, black; it is the **Confused Chaos** <note>original: *Confusum Chaos*; the initial, undifferentiated state of matter at the beginning of the alchemical process.</note>. Because this state signifies **Saturn** <note>Symbol: ♄; representing lead, the heaviest and most "base" metal, and the state of spiritual darkness or melancholy.</note>, who is meant to beget the gods, he is by nature evil, unfriendly, fragile, lame, and limping. He is also poor and can distribute no gifts; even though he is destined to beget great lords and kings, his current state is one of pitiful beggary. His clothes are thin and not firmly woven, and within them, they must suffer much hardship from the frost. Neither the **Sun** <note>Symbol: ☉</note> nor the **Moon** <note>Symbol: ☽</note> is to be seen in these clothes. 

However, after his death, he leaves behind a magnificent inheritance, so that his children and grandchildren shall have clothing enough. He himself possesses the coarse, worldly earth, yet his children shall possess the paradisiacal—indeed, the angelic—world. As the supreme one and the father of them all, he contains these within his body or circle. At first, they live miserably; their food is coarse, sour, bitter, gnawing, and biting, and not many healing herbs are yet to be found among them. Yet their children already possess more wealth and power, and they shall lack for nothing. 

The **Paradise-water**, however, is not only the impure **Eve** <note>Often used in alchemy to represent the unrefined, volatile "feminine" principle or common mercury.</note>, but the pure **Mary** <note>Representing the "Philosophical Mercury" or the refined, virginal matter that has been purified of all dross.</note>, who does not mix herself with **Adam**—the coarse earth. Therefore, she also begets a much more magnificent birth because she is only spiritually impregnated. When the **Sun** <note>Symbol: ☉</note> sleeps in the water with the **Moon** <note>Symbol: ☽</note>, the Moon gives birth to her child in the clouds. In these vapors, the **Stone** <note>The Philosopher's Stone, the goal of the work.</note> is born. When it begins to work within the vessels, the water holds to the earth and makes it round—that is, flowing. The earth begins to smoke and gives off fiery black clouds; and in these clouds, the

...spirit begotten, which first hovered over the waters in the beginning of the **little world** <note>The "little world" or *Microcosmus* refers to the alchemical vessel or the human being, reflecting the creation of the great universe (Macrocosm).</note>, and appears as a small white cloud. If these clouds vanish into smoke through the unskillfulness of the worker, the **Stone** <note>The Philosopher's Stone.</note> is lost, and there remains only a parched earth. Without this water, the earth cannot be brought to a **viscous moisture** <note>A sticky, oily consistency.</note>, which becomes the **radical moisture** <term>radical moisture: the fundamental, life-sustaining fluid believed to reside within all matter, which can survive the heat of fire without evaporating</term> that remains in the fire. 

The water and the earth are husband and wife, and yet of very contrary natures. The earth—which is taken for **Mars** <note>Symbol: ♂; representing iron or the fiery, active principle.</note> and also for the **Sun** <note>Symbol: ☉; representing gold or the perfected masculine principle.</note>—is of a very fiery, dry, and hot nature. The water—which is taken for the daughter of **Saturn** <note>Symbol: ♄; representing lead or the heavy, base material.</note>, and also as **Luna** <note>The Moon.</note> or **Juno**—is cold and moist. They hate and flee from one another; they are the contrary spouses, as well as the two opposing fighters: **Saturn** ♄ and **Mars** ♂. They are the two dragons, the volatile and the fixed, the **toad and the eagle** <note>Alchemical symbols for the heavy, earthy matter (toad) and the light, airborne spirit (eagle).</note>. 

As soon as the woman touches the man, she begins to rage, bubbles and boils, and the cloud appears, rising up from the vapors. But God, after the creation of the earth, did not immediately let it rain upon the earth; instead, a stream went out to water the garden. One must also follow this example: if the rain comes too early, it becomes a **Great Flood** <note>The *Sündfluth*; an excess of moisture that destroys the alchemical "earth" or solid matter in the vessel.</note>, and the earth is ruined. Thus, this river must moisten the earth; finally, through frequent moistening, there arise violent storm-winds, which crack, crash, thunder, and flash with lightning. Through this, the earth is stirred and the soul is separated from the body. Since one is killed by the woman and becomes volatile with her, the clouds then begin to take on colors: black,

Through this, the spirit escapes in a roar of wind, and the work is irretrievably lost. 

These two adversaries are called the **two dragons**; one has wings, the other has none. The one without wings is the **Earth**; the winged one is the **Water of Mercury** <note>Symbol: ☿; the fluid, transformative principle.</note>. These are now like the **Eagle** and the **Toad** bound together with a chain; the Eagle always flies upward into the heights, while the Toad pulls him back down into the Earth with the chain. 

Take what you tread underfoot <note>A classic alchemical riddle suggesting that the "First Matter" is common, cheap, and overlooked by the ignorant.</note>. If you venture to climb without a ladder, I assure you that you will fall on your head. The ladder is the help provided by the water, with its many rungs—the many stages of **imbibition** <term>imbibition: the process of repeatedly soaking a solid substance in a liquid until it is saturated or transformed</term>. It is the marriage of the **brother and the sister** <note>Alchemical symbols for the pairing of masculine (Sulfur) and feminine (Mercury) principles.</note>, who are given a cup of love to drink; they must often be moistened until they have grown into an inner body. This body is the **Hermaphrodite** <note>Also known as the Rebis; a figure representing the perfect union of opposites, possessing both male and female natures.</note> of both male and female nature, which can marry with everything, and the luminous **mercurial** <note>original: "☿al"</note> water. 

No thing in the entire world possesses a **double sulfur** <note>Sulfur here refers to the internal "fire" or soul of the substance.</note> except for this, and it has two natures: volatile and fixed. I speak no error: it has four **Mercuries** <note>Symbol: ☿</note>. 
*   **First**, it has the simple **Virgin Mercury**. 
*   **Second**, it has the fixed **Virgin Earth**. 
*   **Third**, it has the **Spirit of Mercury**, which is its oil. 
*   **Fourth**, it has **mercurial water**.

The Sun’s fire and its soul, which all appear in a liquid, mercurial <note>original: "liqvorischer merculialischer"</note> form, are very heavy. Then they cannot be separated again; where one wants to go, it drags the other along. They must both be purified to the highest degree, for then they will grow into one body; the upper becoming like the lower and the lower like the upper, the earth to heaven and heaven to earth <note>This is a paraphrase of the "Emerald Tablet," a foundational alchemical text attributed to Hermes Trismegistus</note>, which are the inverted **elements** <note>original: "Elementa"</note>. The earth must bubble and boil, whereby the water surges up and gets bubbles which burst and give off vapors.

...and stinking, is now sweet, lovely, and fragrant; it penetrates all veins and nerves in even the outermost limbs, making the human being young and newly created. Through the rays of its light, it drives out all dark, gloomy spirits within the person; whether the diseases stem from evil spirits, or witchcraft, from the stars <note>original: *Astris*</note>, or the elements, it will nonetheless, through its power of radiation, consume the disease like a lightning bolt. If GOD had not established death as a punishment for sin, mankind would live eternally through it and be subject to no disease or pain. 

Here someone might ask: how can such a healing medicine and balm of life be prepared from poison and death? To this, the answer serves: everything was first created good and wholesome by GOD, but through sin, every pure, heavenly essence was extinguished and retreated inward to the center. It remained outwardly a coarse, earthly subject <note>original: *Subjectum*; the raw matter or physical body of a substance</note>, which produces more poison than healing medicine, for the curse has locked it away, and it is as if dead. However, when it enters the alchemical Work, it becomes even more poisonous, because it is first rightly turned <note>The text ends with the prefix "um-", completed by the catchword "gewendt," meaning "turned" or "overturned." if its many colors do not show themselves spiritually—such as yellow, red, and lightning-blue, like the rainbow—then there will be no true separation of the **pure from the impure** <note>original Latin: *purum ab impuro*</note>. Furthermore, the **Paradise-Water** <note>A term for the highly refined alchemical solvent or "mercury of the philosophers" believed to originate from a primordial, pure state.</note> will not be properly prepared, and it will not yet mix with the impure earth, neither bubbling nor boiling. There will be no winds or storms upon the sea <note>Alchemical metaphors for the volatile gas exchanges and reactions within the sealed glass vessel.</note>, and the work will be entirely unsuccessful and in vain, because no **active principle** <note>original Latin: *Agens*; the masculine, heating, or acting force.</note> nor **passive principle** <note>original Latin: *Patiens*; the feminine, cooling, or receiving matter.</note> is present that can heat itself against the other. Therefore, these will not unite; the water will not congeal with the earth, nor will it **dissolve** <note>original: *solviren*</note> it.

Thus, Jason’s voyage to the island of **Colchis** to fetch the **Golden Fleece**, which the dragon guards, is in vain; if he has no wind, the ship cannot move forward. It would then be better to cancel the voyage until one is better prepared for the journey, has better arranged the necessary things, and corrected one's failings, for everything depends upon this voyage. One must first cross the sea and not fear the raging of the waves and the roaring of the wind, which finally brings the ship to **Port** and into that magnificent, good land, to take the Golden Fleece away from the dragon. Once the multi-headed beast is contested and the prepared soup <note>Likely referring to the alchemical "menstruum" or solvent used to "tame" the raw matter.</note> is poured over them, then Jason plows with **fiery oxen** and breaks the soil so that it is prepared to receive the **dragon's teeth** sown into it, from which warriors grow—mighty giants who all slay one another, so that then

[When] questioned, the entire congregation fell upon their knees, thanked and praised God, for such was a promise of victory against their enemies; but where the <term>Urim and Thummim</term> <note>Sacred objects in the High Priest's breastplate used for divination. They returned and brought no fire, but said they had found a **thick water**; this he commanded them to bring, which then ignited the sacrifices.

Now the question arises: what kind of fire hides itself in a mountain, resolves itself over a long period into a **thick water**, and can be ignited once again by the Sun?

I say our **coagulated fire** and **artificial Electrum** <note>Electrum here refers to a specific alchemical substance or "all-metal," rather than the naturally occurring gold-silver alloy.</note>; this high and great Work has such a humble and poor beginning, yet through labor it is brought so high that it passes from the outer, coarse, dark world into the light and radiance of **Paradise**, and from there into the **angelic** <note>original: "engliſche." The Work, according to its right order, is this: the body, the **red earth**, must be brought into a **liquor** <term>liquor</term> <note>In alchemy, a "liquor" is a liquid substance produced when a solid is dissolved into its essential moisture.</note> through its own water, which becomes the foundation of **Chaos** <term>Chaos</term> <note>The "Prima Materia" or first matter; a confused, undifferentiated state of the substance before it is purified.</note>; the red earth holds our **Gold** <note>original symbol: ☉. From this Chaos, the **elements** must be separated; the purified elements are then put back together, and the newborn child is sustained with milk, fed, and watered until it reaches full maturity. The first composition of the red earth is not yet a **conjunction** <term>conjunction</term> <note>The "alchemical marriage" or the union of opposing principles.</note> in the raw work, for there water and **Sulfur** <note>original symbol: 🜔</note> are put together, and these must be separated once more. The second is the true composition of **Water and Earth**; this is **Man and Woman**. The third composition is in **Spirit, Soul, and Body**. The fourth is when the water dries up within the earth and is revived again by the **Air**; once it has thoroughly permeated the earth, then the **Sulfur** <note>original symbol: 🜔</note> can allow itself to be mixed therein, so that it becomes **red blood**. ...found. Indeed, if we have followed our **Redeemer** through the **Cross** and **Death**, and have lived here in His spirit, then we shall come to Him as His brothers and children of **GOD** where He is—namely, at the right hand of **GOD**. He sees how all these things perform His will, except for "rational" man, who opposes **GOD**, even though **GOD** created him so magnificently, did so much good for him, threw everything under his feet, and made him lord over it all. But whoever finds this **mystery** without any understanding or reflection, by whatever means it may happen, let him not use it like a beast, nor live like cattle, nor make it common <note>original: "gemein." In this context, it means to profane or treat a sacred divine secret as something ordinary or worldly.</note>, but instead praise and laud **GOD**, and serve his poor neighbor with it, and not misuse it. Yet another **mystery**, which cannot be left unrevealed: when the **seven forms of nature** <note>In the mystical tradition of Jacob Böhme, these are the seven fundamental properties that constitute all of existence, from the dark "desire" to the light "essentiality."</note> have passed by and reached their completion in the **seventh degree**, then it is the pure **Paradisiacal body**, which is newly strengthened by the **Sea of Oil**—the **spirit of the tincture** <note>original: "Oehl-Meer" and "Geist der Tinctur." In this cosmology, the "inner principle" is the spiritual reality of God's light, as opposed to the "outer principle" of the material world.</note>. There it is subject to no darkening, but remains eternally immortal. 

So also must man gradually put to death his **seven source-spirits** <note>original: "Quel-Geistern." ...to God ceases, so that we hide from God with Adam, just when we should seek Him most, and show ourselves to Him as to the High Priest <note>A reference to the Mosaic Law where lepers had to show themselves to the priest to be declared clean; here, Christ is the High Priest who heals the soul.</note>, so that we might be cleansed of our leprosy of sin <term>leprosy of sin: original "Sünden-Aussatz," a metaphor comparing the corrupting nature of sin to a physical disease that isolates one from the community</term>. We should not be sorry merely because we have deserved hell with our sins—for that we have justly earned—but it must grieve us that we have fallen away from our great Creator, angered Him as our dear Father, and lived contrary to His will. This is true repentance <note>original: "rechte Busse." The author distinguishes between "attrition" (fear of punishment) and "contrition" (sorrow for offending God).</note>, and not the fear of hell; it is also learning to hate, flee, and avoid sin, so that God will be merciful to us for Christ’s sake.

Now it remains to speak of the time when this work of ours can be raised from the outer Principle of the external world into the inner Principle <term>Principle: "Principio," a term used by mystics like Jacob Böhme to describe the fundamental realms of existence (Darkness, Light, and the Visible World)</term>, the World of Light into Paradise, and finally into the angelic and heavenly world. I say it is possible, though such artists <note>original: "Künstler." In this context, it refers to spiritual alchemists or "adepts" who have mastered the internal transformation of the soul.</note> are very rare; yet there is a way to it, though most must plague themselves with the boiling away of the water. ...[must] snatch away from these <term>source-spirits</term> <note>original: "Quel-Geistern"; in the philosophy of Jacob Boehme, these are the seven active qualities or forces that constitute both nature and the human soul</note>, from the world and the <term>outer principle</term> <note>the material, physical world</note>, until they <term>in-qualify</term> <note>original: "inqualiren"; to work, vibrate, or harmonize within a specific quality or nature</note> with all others back into **God**. This is necessary because for many years their souls have lain desolate like the temple of God, and neither sacrifice nor burnt offering has been performed within them. Instead, the soul has been given over to the heathens as an <term>abomination of desolation</term> <note>a biblical reference to the desecration of the Temple, here used as a metaphor for a soul corrupted by worldly desires</note>, where the holy city of God is trampled, where neither temple nor altar is cleansed, and where false idols, one's own images, self-love, and love of the world are not cast out. How then can **Christ** and the Holy Spirit come to them and make a dwelling with them? How can the Holy Spirit enlighten, sanctify, and transfigure the soul so that **Christ** can rise within the soul?

Yet there are some who, quickly and swiftly with their great and earnest repentance, have snatched their soul and their seven source-spirits—which until now had operated in the world—away from the world with force, purified and cleansed them, and turned toward **God** with great repentance and faith. We see this in the <term>thief</term> <note>original: "Schächer"; referring to the "Good Thief" crucified alongside Jesus</note>, when he said: **Lord**, remember me when you come into your kingdom; he received thereupon the comforting answer: Today you shall be with me in Paradise. If he had not possessed the right, true repentance and a strong faith in **Christ**, he would not have received those comforting words, nor would he have believed Him. 

There are three things in heaven that bear witness: the Father, the Word, and the Holy Spirit; the Spirit, the Water, and the Blood, and these three are one. So it is also in our **Work**: Soul, Spirit, and Body—represented by the ☉ <note>The Sun: representing the Soul or Gold</note>, ♁ <note>The Earthly Orb: representing the Body, Salt, or the material matrix</note>, and ☿ <note>Mercury: representing the Spirit or the fluid mediator</note>. These three are also one, and all hidden in one...

This **Subject** <note>original: "Subjectum"; in alchemical philosophy, this refers to the "prime matter" or the fundamental substance of the soul that is being transformed</note> accords with Nature and the Creature; one sees it in man: Adam from the red earth, and from him the woman—both in **God’s image**, pure, perfect, adorned as our **Mercury** <note>symbolized by ☿; in this context, it represents the pure, volatile spirit of the human soul before it was "fixed" or hardened by sin</note> with the virginal wreath of righteousness and holiness. We have examples in birds and fish, which have no such members <note>referring to external genitalia similar to mammals</note>, yet they still multiply. However, they were lustful for the world, began to love it and become infatuated with it, though they should have loved nothing but **God** and remained in simplicity and humility. Thus they tasted the fruits of this world; in that very moment, the world took shape within them and clothed them in the animalistic likeness: the stomach and intestines, with ravenous teeth and animalistic, lustful members, the fruit of which they had tasted. And in that very same moment, they died away from the **Divine Image**; their beautiful crown or wreath fell from them. They knew nothing of **God** or Paradise; they became aware of their animalistic bodies, that they were naked, made themselves aprons, and hid out of shame and fear, and must now, as punishment for their sins, endure death and all the misfortune in the world. Then it is transported into Paradise, shining and sparkling, wherein is no poison nor death, nor any more fading, because it has been washed by the water in the pure element and become snow-white. ...pure, clean spirits of light, transparent, luminous, burning and sparkling, everything just as it was before the **Fall**. In man before the Fall, it was also thus, but after the Fall, nothing remains to man but the **animalistic body** <note>original: "thierische Leib"; the physical body prone to corruption</note>, earth, and filth. If man, as he is now, were to be reunited with **GOD**, a dual nature had to exist—Divine and Human—in the form of the Son of God, a **Mediator**, who united the two opposing natures.

Thus, in this **Work** <note>the "Great Work" or *Magnum Opus* of alchemy</note>, **Mercury** <term>☿</term> is the **heavenly water**, which does not mix with the impure earth, but rather with the pure, clean **Mary** <note>referring both to the Virgin Mary and the alchemical "pure earth" or matrix</note>. Because this water is itself heavenly and spiritual, it makes such earth like unto itself; thus, in its nature, the earth becomes both earthly and heavenly. Without this entrance or means, the pure spirit cannot unite with the body, nor have any entrance into it. The water—the **Mercury** <term>☿</term>—is the **medium conjungendi** <note>Latin: "medium of joining"; the substance that allows two incompatible things to bond</note>.

Furthermore, GOD could not have suffered had He not taken human nature upon Himself, for He was not tangible <note>original: "begreifflich"; literally "graspable" or "comprehensible"</note> and had no body except that which He took from Mary. If this had not occurred through **Christ**, we humans would have had to remain stinking **brands of hell**, and would nevermore have been united with GOD. Thus Christ is our **Mediator** and **Throne of Grace**, who can **tincture** <note>original: "tingiren"; in alchemy, to "tincture" is to permeate and transform a substance's inner nature</note> us through His blood and make us like unto Him.

And just as the **Tincture** tinctures the metallic bodies and turns them into **Gold** <term>☉</term> <note>symbolized by the Sun; the perfect metal</note>, so also Christ—since He took our flesh and blood upon Himself and became like us in the flesh—has made us capable [of being]...

They are simple, pious, righteous, holy **little spirits** <note>original: *Geisterlein*; a diminutive used here to express the lightness, purity, or ethereal nature of celestial beings</note>, and wait daily for **GOD’S** command, which they perform with joy, praising, glorying, exalting, and thanking **GOD** without ceasing, for therein consists their life and joy. 

We wish to follow these holy **little spirits** in the praise and thanks of **GOD**, here with our heavy tongues, until we there spread his praise with angelic tongues and sing the **thrice-Holy** <note>original: *Sanctus*; referring to the "Holy, Holy, Holy" hymn of the liturgy</note>. Here, however, we must fight bravely as knights and warriors of the **Lord Christ** against sin, our own flesh and blood, the devil, and the godless world, until we overcome all enemies of the **Lord Christ** through **Christ** in us and, through distress and death, press through to him; because through his painful suffering and death he has torn us from the jaws of hell and death, where otherwise there would have been no salvation and

<meta>catchword: us</meta>

<summary>This page describes the incomprehensible majesty of God, using the metaphor of a spoon attempting to empty the sea. It exhorts the reader to emulate the ceaseless praise of the angels while remaining steadfast in the spiritual struggle against sin and the world.</summary>
<keywords>omnipotence, angels, archangels, Sanctus, spiritual warfare, Christ, salvation, majesty of God</keywords>

...[who] had to purchase us through his blood, and has given us the Sacraments <note>original: *Sacramenta*; the formal religious rites of the church</note> as a pledge and seal, so that we might rely upon them as his eternal faithfulness and grace. We should nevermore forget his great love, but love him in return as long as we live in the body, so that we may remain eternally enclosed in his love there [in heaven]. May God the Father, Son, and Holy Spirit help us in this, highly beloved and praised in eternity, Amen.

# Conclusion
## of this first treatise.

In heaven, the Sun of Righteousness is the Son of GOD, Jesus Christ, who illuminates all heavenly crowns, angels, and holy people. From him they receive their life, light, and power; without this divine light, everything would be dark and dead, devoid of light, love, and grace. In the macrocosm <note>original: *grossen Welt* (the great world); a term used in this period to describe the universe as a whole</note> is the sun which illuminates all the stars, as well as the entire world. It is the spirit of the whole world, from which the world receives its power, light, life, growth, and fertility, as if from its own life-giving spirit; for without the sun or its radiance, everything would be dead, dark, and frozen. In our microcosm <note>original: *kleiner Welt* (the small world); a philosophical term for the human being as a miniature reflection of the universe</note>, there is also our sun, which illuminates everything, makes it alive, and provides life and growth. Without this internal sun, or its warmth, fire, and light, everything would be dead, lacking power, life, and tincture <term>Tinctur: a spiritual or alchemical essence thought to imbue matter with life, color, and virtue</term>. The first sun, the Son of GOD, the heart...

<summary>This page concludes the first treatise with a trinitarian blessing and introduces a mystical comparison between the divine Sun (Christ), the physical Sun (the spirit of the universe), and the internal Sun of the human soul.</summary>

<keywords>Sacraments, Sun of Righteousness, macrocosm, microcosm, tincture, Jesus Christ, spiritual light, alchemy</keywords>

[The Son] of God is born of God from eternity, and has gone forth into heavenly thrones, particularly into the throne of Lucifer, who was the most beautiful angel, and created from the light of the Son of God. The sun of the macrocosm <note>original: *grossen Welt*; the universe as a whole</note> went forth from the Source-Spirits <term>Quel-Geister: a term from Jacob Boehme's mysticism describing the fundamental "fountain-spirits" or qualities from which all existence originates</term> of the macrocosm on the 4th day in the midst of the seven spirits, as the light that shall illuminate the entire earth. This light God created from the four elements, as from the quintessence <term>Quint-Essenz: the "fifth essence"; the purest and most essential part of a substance, believed to be the matter of the celestial bodies</term> of the pure elements. 

In our microcosm <note>original: *kleinen Welt*; the human being as a miniature reflection of the universe</note>, our sun is also chosen from the midst of the Source-Spirits as the light and heart, a quintessence of the four elements—pure, clear, heavenly, light, power, and life. 

Paradise was an intermediate place in which Adam was set, to test whether he would stand or fall; and it still exists, for God has placed the Cherub <note>A powerful angelic guardian</note> before it to guard the way to the Tree of Life. Our Redeemer also says of the thief <note>original: *Schächer*; the "Good Thief" crucified alongside Jesus</note>: "Today you will be with me in Paradise." Paul was caught up into the third heaven, into Paradise, and heard unspeakable words; it is only that we cannot see it with our animal eyes <note>original: *thierischen Augen*; physical or carnal senses belonging to the mortal, earthly nature</note>. 

It is just as it was there with the Prophet's servant <note>A reference to Elisha and his servant in 2 Kings 6</note>; when he saw that his master and he were besieged, he said: "Alas, what shall we do?" But the Prophet prayed to God: "Lord, open his eyes." Then he saw nothing but fiery chariots and horses, a host of holy angels around himself and his master. Thus our eyes are also not opened to see Paradise until our animal body [passes] through [death] <note>The catchword "Todt" at the bottom indicates the next page begins with the word "Death"</note>.
