...cut [self] off from death, for then we can find Paradise and the Tree of Life, Jesus Christ, eat from it, and live eternally. The reason why God placed Adam in Paradise as an intermediate place <note>original: *Mittel-Ort*; a middle ground or "center-place" between the spiritual and material realms</note> was so that he could be helped again. This was because God clearly saw the envy of the Devil—how he would treat the new creature with hostility and move him toward apostasy. Thus, God created him from the quintessence <term>Quint-Essenz: the "fifth essence"; the purest and most essential part of matter, beyond the four physical elements</term> of the earth, a red loam or clay. This lump was moistened into a mass with the blessed, pure, sweet light-water from the stars <note>original: *Astris*; referring to the celestial or astral influences</note>. 

This creature, man, was intended to be the Paradisical body, the image created after God, a pure and unblemished mirror in which God wished to behold Himself as a father does in his son. Had he remained steadfast, he would have been transfigured into an angel like Lucifer through the light of the Son of God, which would have illuminated him and immediately transformed his Paradisical body into an angelic one. Had he fallen after that point, it would have been impossible to help him again, just as it was with Lucifer. But this time, God held back His help; He wanted to demonstrate His divine justice and omnipotence, showing that He could cast down the evil angels while yet saving and liberating poor, repentant man. This Adam was intended to rule over the stars and over the macrocosm <note>original: *grosse Welt*; the universe as a whole</note>, to produce an angelic host from within himself—nothing but holy children of God. He was not to love the stars <note>original: *Astra*</note> or the outer world, neither nature nor creature, nor even...

...[him]self, to desire, know, will, and love nothing but **GOD**. He was not meant to eat the earthly fruits of this world, but rather Angel-Bread <note>original: *Engel-Brodt*; spiritual nourishment intended for celestial beings</note>, the fruits of Paradise from the pure and holy elements. But he became lustful for the fruits of the great world, and went back again into the mother’s womb <note>referring to the material world or the earthly elements from which his physical form was extracted</note> from which he had gone into Paradise. Instead of being translated into the angelic world as intended, he went backward; he ate of the earth and earthly fruits. As soon as he tasted these, he became a beast, for he took on the earthly form instead of the angelic one. 

The Devil, when he saw that man was half-conquered by lust, also employed his cunning. He knew well that pride <term>Hoffart: a specific type of spiritual arrogance or "high-mindedness" that seeks to elevate oneself above God</term> and wanting to be equal to **GOD** would be the greatest sin, for it was through this that he himself had fallen. He saw the Tree of Temptation <note>original: *Versuch-Baum*; the Tree of the Knowledge of Good and Evil</note> with the beautiful fruits of the world, and deceived the humans into believing that if they ate of it, their eyes would be opened and they would be like **GOD**. 

Our first parents believed this—who at that same time were still in nothing but pure simplicity and innocence—and they ate of the fruits of the world, even though **GOD** had commanded: "On the day you eat of it, you shall surely die." The lying Devil, however, said: "You shall not surely die." Now, they did not recognize the Devil, for he did not appear to them in the form of a devil, but spoke through the serpent—which before the curse had been a beautiful creature. They ate of it, and in that same hour the Great Spirit of the World <note>original: *Welt-Geist*; the governing spirit of the physical, temporal universe</note> fashioned them; after they had become lustful and had eaten of its fruit, it clothed them in the animal image; they

...spirits <note>original: "ster"; likely the conclusion of the word *Geister* (spirits) from the previous page.</note> also consist in the animal spirit <term>animal spirit<note>A term from early modern physiology and psychology referring to the vital life-force or "spirits" that animate the body and mediate between the soul and the physical senses.</note></term>, and through this, he [the Devil] has an influence on our heart; but when our spirits co-qualify <term>co-qualify<note>original: *inqualiren*; a technical term in Böhmean mysticism meaning to work together or share the same inner qualities or "qualifications."</note></term> with **GOD** and enlighten our animal spirit, then the sidereal spirits <term>sidereal spirits<note>original: *syderischen Geister*; the astral or "star-spirits" within the human constitution that connect the individual to the celestial influences of the macrocosm.</note></term> become quite fervent and eager for the Divine Light.

There the Devil rages and fumes, for he has his seat in the outer birth of man. He cannot endure this Divine Light; it was once his, and it was taken from him. He begrudges it to man, raging and fuming, wanting to ignite all the <term>source-spirits<note>original: *Quel-Geister*; in the mysticism of Jacob Böhme, these are the seven fundamental "fountain-spirits" or driving forces of all reality.</note></term> into wrath, so that they should burn in the Wrath of God. This is something the "new man" in Christ will not suffer, extinguishing [the fire] in the "old Adam" <note>The "Old Adam" represents the fallen, sinful nature of humanity, while the "New Man" represents the soul reborn through spiritual transformation.</note> within the realm of wrath. Then the battle begins: Michael and his angels fight with the dragon, and this struggle lasts until our death.

If we overcome through the new birth, which stands in **God** within Paradise, then after death we are also inside it. But if the old Adam—the sinful birth through which we stand in the wrath and the Devil's kingdom—overcomes us, then we belong to that kingdom after death as well. Throughout this entire life, we stand with one foot in the paradisiacal World of Light, and with the other in the dark World of Wrath, the kingdom of the Devil and of Hell. When man triumphs and overcomes through Christ, he sees the danger in which he was stuck and learns to guard himself against the Devil, and in death he is entirely separated from him; but if the Devil overcomes, and the soul surrenders to the world and its life of vice, then after death man is in the Kingdom of Hell and is the Devil’s bondservant.

<header>❀ ):( o ):( ❀</header>

From this also comes the bodily possession of the Devil, when he makes his **robber-castle** <note>original: *Raub-Schloß*; a vivid metaphor common in 17th-century mysticism for the devil’s illegal occupation of the human soul which rightfully belongs to God.</note> within man. From this we see that **Paradise** and **Hell** are also within us, in which we live here; in them we are in death. If we live in the **new birth** <term>new birth<note>original: *neue Gebuhrt*; a central concept in the theology of Jacob Böhme and later Pietism, referring to the radical spiritual transformation where a person is "born again" into a divine state.</note></term>, in the new man, then we need not seek Paradise far away, for the **Kingdom of God is within us**.

But if we live in the old sinful birth, then we also need not seek Hell far away, for we already live inside it, and as soon as the godless soul leaves the body, the Devil has it in his hands. Christ has the greatest claim on the soul, for first of all, **God** created it; when it fell through sin into the realm of Death and Hell, the Son of God became man to redeem it, suffered the bitter death, descended into Hell, and took the captive souls from the captor, the Devil. That is to say, he led the captives out of the prison <note>original: *er hat die Gefangene aus dem Gefängniß gefangen geführet*; a reference to Psalm 68:18 and Ephesians 4:8, depicting Christ as a conquering hero liberating prisoners of war.</note>, and distributed the plunder; he rescued them from the pit where there was no water <note>A reference to Zechariah 9:11, often interpreted in mystical Christianity as the liberation of souls from the "dry" state of spiritual death.</note>. We now belong to him who conquered and won us through death, strife, battle, and victory, and who also laid down his life for us out of love. The realm of Death and Hell will not be able to hold us; Christ has swallowed up death in victory. **Death, where is your sting? Hell, where is your victory?** Thanks be to **GOD**, who has given us the

-> victory, through our <-
-> Lord JESUS <-
-> Christ! <-

<summary>This page describes Paradise and Hell as internal spiritual states present within the human soul during life. It concludes with an exaltation of Christ's victory over death and the devil, liberating the soul from its internal "prison."</summary>
<keywords>internal paradise, internal hell, new birth, kingdom of God, redemption, spiritual warfare, Christ's victory, Jacob Böhme</keywords>

# The Second Treatise.

This **Stone** <note>original: *Stein*; in this mystical and alchemical context, the "Stone" refers to the "Philosopher’s Stone" of the soul—the transformed and perfected human spirit or the presence of Christ within the believer.</note> has only two births; the Son of God also has two births: one from eternity, the other in time; one Divine and one Human, in the womb of Mary. Man also has two births: one sinful, the other through Baptism—of water and Spirit. Now, if man is to walk in a new life, he must be born again through the Spirit; for even though the outer man is baptized with the water-baptism, while the inner man is baptized with the **holy element** <term>holy element<note>original: *heiligen Element*; in the theosophy of writers like Jacob Böhme, this refers to a pure, spiritual substance—the "heavenly materiality"—that stands in contrast to the four fallen physical elements.</note></term>, he still produces no "new man" after baptism. Instead, he produces the **old Adam** <term>old Adam<note>A traditional theological term for the fallen, sinful nature of humanity inherited from the first man, Adam.</note></term>, who is quick and clever for all godless living, but lazy and sluggish toward what is good, from childhood even into old age. 

If he is now to turn around and "put on the new man," he must be born again through the Spirit, crucifying and killing the old Adam so that he is drowned daily with all sins and evil desires, and a new man may emerge, who lives eternally before God in righteousness and purity. Water washes away **original sin** <term>original sin<note>original: *Erb-Sünde*; the hereditary stain of sin believed to be passed down to all humans from Adam and Eve.</note></term>,

The Spirit, however, penetrates the entire new man, takes away all stains, and renews the person in spirit, soul, and body, as the Apostle says:

> May the God of peace sanctify you through and through, and may your whole spirit, soul, and body be kept blameless until the coming of our Lord Jesus Christ. <note>A translation of 1 Thessalonians 5:23.</note>

By this, he means the **Fire-Baptism** <term>Fire-Baptism<note>original: *Feuer-Tauffe*; a mystical concept referring to a profound spiritual purification by the Holy Spirit that goes beyond the initial ritual of water baptism.</note></term> through the Holy Spirit, which is our enlightenment and **transfiguration** <note>original: *Verklährung*; this refers to the spiritual process of being "clothed" in divine light or restored to a glorified, heavenly state.</note>. We have an example of this in the disciples of the Lord Christ: although they lived as children of God in the "new man," had Christ personally with them, and also believed in him, they were still children in divine knowledge. The Lord Christ had to open their understanding after his resurrection; yet they still possessed doubt and unbelief—pure imperfection. They still fell occasionally into sin, as Peter did, and could not yet perform any miracles until after the Ascension of the Lord Christ, when the Holy Spirit was poured out over them. After that, they were the miracle-workers and holy apostles through the Holy Spirit; they performed all the same miracles as the Son of God himself and confirmed their pure teaching through their blood, which they shed as joyful martyrs. Of this baptism, John says:

> I baptize with water; he who comes after me will baptize you with the Holy Spirit and with fire. <note>A translation of Matthew 3:11.</note>

All faithful, pious children of God should have this baptism, for it is the Spirit of our transfiguration, through which we are transformed into angels, which [awaits] us in Paradise

...this was reserved. Had Adam and Eve been enlightened by the Holy Spirit in their paradisiacal bodies and then fallen, they would have met the same fate as Lucifer, and would have remained apostates from God. However, God wished to preserve humanity to spite the Devil; He allowed man to be tempted in his paradisiacal, untransfigured <term>untransfigured<note>original: *ohnverklärten*; in this mystical context, it refers to a state that is pure and innocent but has not yet been fully permeated and "clothed" in the permanent, radiant glory of God's light.</note></term> body. In that state, man was a sheer, pure human—innocent, righteous, clean, chaste, simple, and pious. He knew nothing but God and saw and recognized nothing but God, until the "Great World" <note>The "Great World" or Macrocosm refers to the physical, material universe and its earthly desires.</note> with its fruits—the **Tree of Temptation**—presented itself. Then, he grew lustful for the fruits of the world, which were nevertheless deadly, and through this, he fell into sin and death.

All people desire to possess the Holy Spirit and be enlightened by Him, and God is willing to grant the Holy Spirit to those who ask. Yet, we must first pray: *Create in me, O God, a clean heart, and give me a new, steadfast spirit.* <note>A quotation of Psalm 51:10.</note> It is the greatest grace from God that a mortal can attain: to tear oneself away from sin here, to live in the spirit of Christ, and to be enlightened by the Holy Spirit in this life. 

But there is also a great danger in this: if such a person turns back, he has "laid his hand to the plow" and then pulled it away again; he is not fit for the Kingdom of God. <note>A reference to Luke 9:62.</note> It would have been better for him never to have recognized the right path than to be entangled again in his former sins; for such a person, the last state has become worse than the first. <note>A reference to 2 Peter 2:20-21.</note> Christ died for sinners once and for all,

Now, if such a person has been raised with Christ <note>original: "Christo" (Latin dative case commonly used in German theological texts)</note> from the death of sins, has walked in a new life with Christ, and is anointed and enlightened by the Holy Spirit, the Apostle speaks of this once again:

> It is impossible for those who were once enlightened, and have tasted the heavenly gifts, and have become partakers of the Holy Spirit, and have tasted the good word and the powers of the world to come, if they should fall away, to be renewed again unto repentance, seeing they crucify the Son of God for themselves all over again and hold Him up to mockery. <note>A paraphrase of Hebrews 6:4–6.</note>

This is the **Sin against the Holy Spirit**, <term>Sin against the Holy Spirit<note>A theological concept based on Matthew 12:31-32, referring to a state of heart where one willfully rejects the known truth of God’s grace, making repentance impossible.</note></term> for the Spirit of Grace is insulted, with whom he was nonetheless sealed; this sin is forgiven neither in this world nor in the next. Therefore, one must pray:

> Take not Your Holy Spirit from me. <note>A quotation of Psalm 51:11.</note>

We have an example in the enlightened **Solomon**: how he fell, yet we do not read that he ever received the Spirit of God again; for where that same Spirit has once established His dwelling and is then driven out, He never returns again. We see this in the fall of **Lucifer**; had he not been enlightened by the light of God, he could not have fallen so deep. But because man was not yet enlightened when he fell, he could be helped again through the Son of God, through the taking on of our nature—which, being restored through His divine nature, cleanses our "new man" so that he may be enlightened by the Holy Spirit. To be **taught by God** when the Anti-Christ is discovered—that son of perdition, the beast, and the false prophet—then the Holy Spirit is indeed double, even triple within us; for He proceeds from the Father, Son, and Holy Spirit. We then no longer see Him as the **wrath-zealous God**, as when He gave the Law with thunder and lightning, but rather as it was with the prophet: after the wind had passed, there came a **still, gentle whistling**. <note>A reference to 1 Kings 19:11–12, where Elijah finds God not in the wind, earthquake, or fire, but in a "still small voice" (German: *stilles sanfftes Sausen*).</note> No one can or will believe any of this unless they have had, through the Holy Spirit, a brief and small glimpse into the **Divine Mystery**, where all at once their heart is illuminated by the Divine Light and they have felt this pure, gentle, igniting fire in their soul. This cannot be described with any pen, and the joy of this Divine Light in the soul is unspeakable; it illuminates all people toward eternal life.

Now I will also describe the births of our **Paradise Stone**; <term>Paradise Stone<note>original: "Paradieß-Stein"; in alchemical and mystical literature, this refers to the Philosopher’s Stone or the perfected "new man" in Christ, representing the restoration of the soul to its original, unfallen state.</note></term> its birth is like that of man from the **red earth**, <note>original: "rothen Erden"; a reference to the Hebrew name *Adam*, which is etymologically linked to *adāmah* (earth/ground) and often associated with the color red.</note> out of which man was created into a Paradisiacal body. <meta>This page continues the alchemical and theological description of the "Paradisical Body" or "Mercury" introduced on the previous page. Springs, which finally become brooks full of pure little fish, pour themselves out seven distinct times into the earth, where they lose themselves and emerge again. This finally becomes a heavenly water, indestructible and imperishable, which grants immortality, gives birth to all things anew, and rules over the elements.

Those former waters only washed away surface dirt, as when coarse uncleanness is washed from a cloth. While the coarse earth remained in a state of **solution** <term>Solution: The alchemical process of dissolving a solid into a liquid, often seen as a step toward purification.</term>, it remained subject to its former nature of **corruption** <term>Corruption: In this context, the state of decay or physical frailty that affects all matter after the Fall of Man.</term> and impotence. Furthermore, the seven **source-spirits** <note>Likely a reference to the seven "Qual-Geister" or fountain-spirits described by the mystic Jacob Böhme, which represent the fundamental forces of divine and natural manifestation.</note> were still stuck in their **confused chaos** <note>original: *Confuso Chao*. A state of primordial, unorganized matter.</note>; they did not yet stand in distinctness, nor had they united into the Light. Therefore, this **Paradise-Water** is very different from the previous coarse elemental water; it is clear, shining, and spotless, separated from its impurity. 

But what fruits, pleasure, and delight does Paradise bring us? It brings us the sweet balsam or sugar of **Saturn** (♄); the golden rain of **Jupiter** (♃); the healing herb that drives away all sorrow and mourning—which has a black root and a white blossom—called **Mercury** (☿) <note>This herb with a black root and white flower is a reference to "Moly," a magical plant from Greek mythology given by Hermes (Mercury) to Odysseus to protect him from magic.</note>; the love of **Venus** (♀), her honey and balsam, her green forest where the Bull and the Ram graze and the doves of **Diana** <note>In alchemy, Diana (the moon goddess) often symbolizes the "white" or silver stage of the process, associated with purity.</note> fly; the sugar and heavenly bread of **Luna** (the Moon), the marzipan, the nectar-drink brewed from honey and milk, and **ambrosia**, the food of the gods. This is the heavenly water of life, the vegetative essence in which the metals grow, and the great circulation.

This refers to a high-level alchemical process of continuous distillation or a "universal medicine" that has been fully refined.</note>, Juno with her peacock, Jason with the Golden Fleece, and all the gods in their most precious joy. **Mercury** (☿) is the messenger for them all; he brings them letters of delicate, thin linen or fine gauze, signaling that the time of the **wedding** <note>The "Chymical Wedding," the union of opposites (usually the King/Sun and Queen/Moon) to produce the Philosopher's Stone.</note> is now at hand. 

Paradise is built from the small earth, which was taken from the previous **red earth** out of its **center** <note>original: *Centro*. This alludes to the creation of Adam from red clay, mirrored in the alchemical search for the "virgin earth."</note>, just as the woman was taken from the man. It is filled with the middle water until it reaches a crystalline clarity, from which a fountain has sprung. The **Water of Life** that gushes from it is like the stream that flowed from the Temple and was measured [in the vision of] the prophet Ezekiel, until it finally became so deep that it could not be fathomed. This signifies eternity, because through this water one proceeds toward eternity and is glorified **unto infinity** <note>original: *in infinitum*</note>. This stream was measured by a man who appeared as if made of bronze, as described in the Prophet Ezekiel, where all human understanding must fall silent before this great work.

From this, mankind has learned to know the Son of God: how he had to be born of a virgin, how he had to suffer and feel the serpent's sting, and how he overcame the serpent—the devil—and destroyed his kingdom. The whole of Holy Scripture points us to this work as if with fingers. Whoever has the **Key of David** <note>A biblical reference (Revelation 3:7) signifying the power to open what no one can shut; in this context, it refers to the divine insight required to unlock the secrets of nature and scripture.</note> from God understands it; but whoever does not have such grace from God, though they may be enlightened by Him in the light of nature...

## The Fourth Treatise.
### Of the Universal Stone, or Universal Tincture.

Whoever wishes to prepare this should seek the Light; this dwells within man, in the understanding, and also in all of God’s creatures. Let man pray to GOD, and He will enlighten him through His Divine Light and open his darkened understanding, so that he may first find the **New Man** <note>original: "neuen Menschen"; a theological concept of the spiritually reborn self, here applied to the alchemist's internal state.</note> in the earth—that is, in himself—and within that man find the Kingdom of God and the **precious Pearl**. <note>The "Pearl of Great Price" is a recurring alchemical metaphor for the Philosophers' Stone or the spark of divine spirit hidden in matter.</note> Thereafter, let him seek in the pure metallic earth the precious snow-white Pearl of the **Universal** <term>Universal: Short for the Universal Medicine or Stone, a substance that acts upon all kingdoms of nature (animal, vegetable, and mineral) rather than just one.</term>; then he will see, feel, smell, taste, and grasp what glorious fruits the elements provide: so many wonderful growths of beautiful colors, scent, and taste. We see such things in the elements of the Great World: what beautiful fruits the earth has in flowers, herbs, trees, the grapevine, grain, oil, metals, minerals, salts, and countless varieties of fruits, as well as animals! The water has so many kinds of fish; even the birds have their origin in the water and were created from it. <note>This reflects a traditional interpretation of Genesis 1:20, suggesting that birds and fish share a common elemental origin in water.</note> The air is filled with spirits, and all animal-

# The Fourth Treatise.
## Concerning the Universal Stone, or Universal Tincture. <term>Universal Tincture: Also known as the Philosopher's Stone, this was a legendary substance believed to be capable of transmuting base metals into gold and acting as a panacea for all diseases.</term>

Our **matter** <term>Materia: The "Prime Matter" or starting material of the Great Work, which alchemists believed contained the potential for all things.</term> has a fivefold **solution** <note>original: "Solution"; in alchemy, this refers to the process of dissolving a solid substance into a liquid, often seen as a symbolic death or purification of the matter.</note>: First, the main solution; the second, that of the **elements**; the third, the philosophical solution of water and earth; the fourth, through the white **ferment** <note>original: "Ferment"; a substance added to the alchemical mixture to "leaven" or catalyze the transformation, much like yeast in bread-making.</note>, the spirit; the fifth solution through fire, as the soul.

The fifth solution of the white stone into its red oil, by which the stone is turned red; this is the "chariot of the soul," where the **Sun** travels upon four wheels. 

Contained within this are three kinds of unions: 
1. The water with the earth.
2. The spirit with the body.
3. The soul with spirit and body.

...the right **composition** <term>Composition: The joining of substances to form a new whole.</term> of Water and Earth; this is **Man and Wife** <note>In alchemy, the union of "Man and Wife" symbolizes the marriage of opposites, such as fixed and volatile substances.</note>. The third is the composition with **Sulphur** <term>Sulphur: The active, masculine principle in alchemy, representing heat and soul.</term>, when there is no more water, but rather everything has dried into the Earth; then the **Sulphur** can unite with the **Mercury** <term>Mercury: The passive, feminine principle, representing fluidity and spirit.</term>. The fourth composition is with **Fire**, when it is said: "Give fire to the fire, and mercury to the mercury," for then you shall possess the riches of fortune. 

From these four elements comes the **Quintessence** <term>Quintessence: The "fifth essence" or purest part of a substance, believed to be the life-force of the universe.</term> in the glass. First, we assemble it and let it **putrefy** <note>original: "verfaulen"; a stage of the work where matter is left to rot or ferment, often turning black (the "nigredo" stage).</note>. That which has putrefied, we **dissolve**; that which is dissolved, we **divide**; that which is divided, we **purify**; that which is purified, we **reunite**; and that which is reunited, we **fix** <note>original: "figiren"; to "fix" a substance means to make it stable under heat so it no longer evaporates.</note>. 

When, after the **mortification** <note>original: "Tödtung"; the symbolic "killing" or breaking down of the physical substance.</note>, the body is resurrected and reunited with the soul, it becomes the **glorified paradisiacal body**. 

### Practice from the Water

From our simple **Mercury** and its own Earth, our seven metals grow. This **Virgin Mercury** is **sublimated** <note>Sublimation is the process of heating a substance so it turns into vapor and then solidifies again, used here to purify it.</note> seven times with its own Earth until it is released from its wateriness, coming from its moist nature into dryness and maturity, becoming a **white stone** like **Crystal**. This **Water- and Salt-Stone** is sublimated into the **foliated earth** <note>original: "terram foliatam"; a stage where the refined matter appears as thin, leaf-like scales.</note>. 

The **double mercury** is **calcined** <note>Calcined: reduced to a powder or ash through intense heat.</note> with the greatest fire; for one must not rely on the sublimated mercury, but rather on that which is fixed in its own fire and its own **Oil**, which fixes and calcines it. Thus the Stone is ready: the **glassy sea** <note>A reference to the transparency and purity of the finished alchemical work, echoing the Book of Revelation.</note>. 

If one now wishes to **multiply** <term>Multiplication: The process of increasing the power, weight, or quantity of the Philosopher's Stone.</term> it in quantity and quality, one must **dissolve** the **crowned White Queen** into the **flying Crowned Eagle**, and let our **Red Sun** descend into this well, so

* ):( ( o ):( *

How to bring life and growth to the dark, dead body follows in this brief report. Our **matter** <term>Matter: In German "Materia," the raw starting substance of the alchemical work.</term> has a five-fold **dissolution** <term>Dissolution: In German "Solution," the process of breaking down a solid into liquid form.</term>: First, the Main **Dissolution**; second, that of the elements; third, the philosophical **dissolution** of Water and Earth; fourth, through the white **ferment** <term>Ferment: A substance that "leavens" or transforms the matter, much like yeast in bread.</term>, the Spirit; the fifth **dissolution** is through Fire, as the Soul. 

The first Main **Dissolution** is a **reduction** <note>original: "Reduction"; the process of returning a solid body to its liquid or "prime" state.</note> of the perfect body into a **liquid**. The second **dissolution** of the elements is a separation of the parts from **chaos** <note>original: "Chaos"; the primal, unorganized state of matter before the alchemist imposes order.</note> through **distillation** into Fire, Water, Air, and Earth. The third **dissolution** is an unlocking of **Man and Woman** <note>In alchemy, "Man and Woman" or "Husband and Wife" represent the pairing of opposites, such as the fixed and the volatile.</note>. 

The fourth **dissolution** of the **ferment** is a **reduction** of the white sulfur into its white oil. The fifth **dissolution** is of the white stone into its red oil, by which the stone is turned red; this is the chariot of the souls, upon which the **Sun drives on four wheels** <note>The "four wheels" typically represent the four elements or the four main stages of the work that carry the "Sun" (the completed Gold or Red Stone) to its destination.</note>.

Within this are contained three kinds of unions: 
1. The Water with the Earth.
2. The Spirit with the Body.
3. The Soul with Spirit and Body. 

The labor for such things is to bring the bodies into a **liquid** called **hyle** <term>Hyle: A Greek term for "primordial matter," the most basic substance of the universe.</term> or **chaos**, to separate the **elements** from that **chaos**, to put the purified **elements** back together, and to nourish, feed, and water the **child with milk** <note>The "Alchemical Child" is a metaphor for the growing Stone; "milk" refers to the gentle liquid nutrients (often mercurial) used to "grow" it.</note> so that it reaches full maturity and can be **fermented**. 

Regarding the composition, it happens thus: The first is not a true **conjunction** <term>Conjunction: The chemical and spiritual marriage of substances.</term> in the raw work, for there this Water and Sulfur 🜍 <note>Alchemy symbol for Sulfur</note> are merely put together. These must be separated again. The second is the

# The Fifth and Final Treatise,
## Wherein six figures from the Revelation
## of John are interpreted, first in a spiri-
## tual manner, and then also according to 
## the natural work.

To interpret the **Revelation of John** <note>The final book of the New Testament, also known as the Apocalypse, which many alchemists believed contained hidden chemical instructions.</note> requires a person who understands the **Divine Cabala** <term>Cabala: A mystical tradition used here to decode secret meanings in sacred texts.</term> as well as the **heavenly and angelic Magic**, and **natural Philosophy** along with the **Nature-Language** <term>Nature-Language: The "Natursprache," believed to be the original language of Adam that reveals the true essence of all things.</term>, because the entire Revelation contains particularly many **Magical images and figures** which represent the three realms or three principles:

1. The **Mysterium Magnum** <term>Mysterium Magnum: The "Great Mystery," a term from Jacob Böhme referring to the undifferentiated source of all things in God.</term>, the Divine, Heavenly, and Angelic world.
2. The **Paradisiacal World of Light**.
3. The **outer dark world**.

These three are all contained in **God**, in one circle, yet none comprehends the other, and yet God is in all. All things likewise proceed from Him: the angels, nature, and creatures, including **Man**, who has fallen from God and from the Paradisiacal world into the outer dark

## The First Figure.

### Revelation of John, Chapter 1.

## Spiritual Interpretation of the Third Figure.

Because man, however, did not harmonize <note>original: "inqualirt," a technical term in Böhmean mysticism meaning to work in union with or to be infused with a specific quality.</note> within God through the seven source-spirits, and fell into differentiation <note>original: "Schiedlichkeit," the state of being separated or broken into individual, conflicting parts rather than existing in divine unity.</note>, man was therefore subject to the wrath and judgments of God. These are sealed by seven seals with righteousness and truth, so that no one may contradict Him. The Divine judgments also remain hidden from man, unless He Himself reveals His will to him, and he turns back from differentiation, and these seals are broken one after another. In this way, he may enter again into the new man, harmonizing with God, and be transformed from wrath back into the light and grace of God—yet not through any other means than through Christ. # Spiritual Interpretation of the Third Figure.

And I saw in the right hand of him who sat on the throne a book, written within and on the back, <term>etc. [original: "etc."]</term> He who sat on the throne signifies the Son of God, the ruling King upon this throne. The book in his right hand, written within and on the back, is Nature and the Created Being, according to all seven <term>Source-Spirits: "Quel-Geister," the seven fundamental divine forces in Böhme’s theology that constitute the life of God and the universe</term> and <term>Emanations: "Ausgebuhrten," the outward manifestations or "out-births" of these spiritual forces</term>, written within and on the back, <term>etc.</term>; [it represents] the Divine Governance and Providence, especially regarding humanity, through the Word and Gospel.

But because humanity, through the seven <term>Source-Spirits</term>, did not <term>inter-work with God: "inqualiren," a term describing the way divine qualities should flow together and operate within a person</term>, and instead fell into <term>Differentiation: "Schiedlichkeit," the state of being separated from the divine unity into conflicting or disharmonious qualities</term>, humanity was under the wrath and judgments of God. These are sealed with seven seals with justice and truth, such that no one may contradict Him. 

These Divine judgments are also hidden from man, unless He Himself reveals His will to him, and man turns back from <term>Differentiation</term>, and these seals are broken one after another. In this way, man may enter again into the "new man," <term>inter-working with God</term>, and be transformed out of wrath and back into the light and grace of God—yet not otherwise than through Christ. # Spiritual Interpretation of this Fourth Figure.

This angel from heaven signifies once again the Son of God in His Divine and Human nature, transfigured through the seven <term>source-spirits</term> <note>original: "Quel-Geister," a term from Jakob Böhme’s theosophy referring to the seven fundamental qualities or forces of divine and natural manifestation</note> of God, which have permeated the human nature. He was clothed with the cloud, etc., signifying His majesty and radiance in the seventh <term>out-birth</term> <note>original: "Ausgebuhrt," meaning the outward manifestation or result of an internal spiritual process</note>. And a rainbow was upon his head, etc., surrounded by the spirits of all nature, and united with our human source-spirits, which exist in so many diversities and colors. This union occurs through the assumption of our flesh; despite these diversities and the <term>emanation</term> <note>original: "Ausgehung," literally a "going-out" or proceeding forth</note> from Him, He nevertheless takes us back to Himself in grace and mercy through our repentance, uniting us with His pure, Divine Spirit, and making our spirits like unto His Spirit.

His face shines like the sun, and his feet like pillars of fire, etc. After His resurrection, His human nature is transfigured into the Divine, so that He is also **GOD** within the human nature. The feet like pillars of fire signify humanity, which appeared in the first figure as brass or bronze, but now, after His suffering, death, and resurrection, has been transfigured and purified in Him, like silver refined through the fire seven times. He sets his right foot upon the sea, etc. This represents His divinity, power, and authority, which He holds in heaven and on earth, and as a true **GOD**

## Spiritual Interpretation of this Fifth
## Figure.

The woman clothed with the sun, etc., is the Christian Church, the pure humanity of the new man in the life of **JESUS Christ**, clothed with the sun, with **Christ**, whom she received in baptism. She has the moon under her feet, etc., meaning everything temporal, transitory, etc., and the many diversities and source-spirits <note>original: "Quel-Geister." In the mystical philosophy of Jacob Böhme, these are the "fountain-spirits" or fundamental driving forces of nature that can be either turbulent or harmonious.</note>, which she does not allow to rule over her, and which she treads underfoot. The crown of 12 stars, etc., represents the holy teachers of the Apostles and Prophets.

She has trodden under her feet all seven forms of nature <term>seven forms of nature</term> <note>These are the seven foundational qualities or "properties" (often linked to the seven planets) that constitute the material and spiritual world in theosophical alchemy.</note> and the creaturely state; she has resisted the evil influence and allurements of the planets, as well as her own source-spirits and evil impulses, and has not allowed them to rule over her.

She is pregnant with the new man in Christ and suffers great agony in giving birth before she tears herself away from the world and her own allurements, and Christ takes shape in us; therefore the Church, the Bride of Christ—the inner man—must suffer and endure much. Finally, the child, the inner man, is born through pain and anxiety. This child was caught up to **GOD** and His throne, etc. This new man is the Paradisiacal Image, the child of **GOD**, of the **LORD** **Christ**

## Interpretation of the fifth figure in the great natural work.

After the trumpet of the 7th angel, the nature of this work's end appears: the white tincture, the woman clothed with the sun, the moon under her feet, etc. She is truly the white sun, or the white ☉ <note>Alchemical symbol for Gold or the Sun.</note> which, when fermented with ☉ [gold], will endure all ☉ [gold] tests. She is the chaste queen who has trodden under her feet all the seven source-spirits <note>original: "Quel-Geister." In the philosophy of Jacob Böhme, these are the seven fundamental qualities or forces that drive all of existence.</note> and separations <note>original: "Schiedligkeiten." Refers to the distinct properties or qualities of matter that emerge during the alchemical process.</note> with their many colors—all of which were adulterated <note>Implying the colors were mixed or impure before being unified in the white stage.</note> therein—in the moon's unstable light, and the seven degrees or *systems*. She has mastered them all, making herself regent over them; the sun rests within her in the white tincture.

The sun is still hidden behind the moon, which cannot be clearly seen because of the moon's glow; she is pregnant with the solar fruit. The child that she will bear will be received among the gods, for he is to become the son of the sun <note>In alchemy, the "Son of the Sun" often refers to the perfected Philosopher's Stone or the "Red King" that follows the "White Queen."</note>. To the woman are given the wings of a great eagle, so that she may fly into the wilderness and be nourished there; she is dissolved with the flying crowned eagle, so that she becomes volatile <note>original: "flüchtig." In an alchemical context, this means the substance is transformed into a gaseous or spiritual state, capable of rising or "flying" in the vessel.</note>. The red dragon shoots a stream

# Interpretation of this sixth figure regarding the natural Great Work.

<note>The "Great Work" (Latin: *Magnum Opus*) refers to the alchemical process of transmuting base matter into a perfected state, often symbolized by the Philosopher's Stone.</note>

This sign is great and wonderful, etc. The emerald or the green <term>Leo: (♌) In alchemy, the "Green Lion" typically represents the raw, acidic mineral state or mercury before it is purified.</term>, the sea of glass, etc. The white tincture is mingled with fire, etc.; the seven angels have poured out the bowls of wrath; the three-times sevenfold magic numbers are fulfilled through water, air, and fire, through which it <note>The "it" here refers to the matter being worked upon in the alchemical vessel.</note> was plagued and endured heavy pain. One of the four beasts <note>original: "Thiere." Referring to the four living creatures of the Apocalypse (Revelation 4:6–8), often associated by alchemists with the four elements.</note> gave the seven angels seven bowls full of the wrath of GOD, the final plagues. One of the four beasts is one of the elements, such as fire, by which the matter is plagued and burned when it must drink its own wrath and poison; through this final trial, it becomes entirely fiery red like a glowing coal. The conflict has now subsided; all the wonders of Art <note>In this context, "Art" refers to alchemy, the "Spagyric Art."</note> and Nature have found their end; the uppermost is like the lowermost, and the lowermost is like the uppermost <note>This is a reference to the famous Hermetic maxim from the *Emerald Tablet*: "That which is below is as that which is above."</note>. The beginning has found the end, and the end the beginning. The many spirits and diversities that proceeded from it, are

That was great and wonderful. Finally, this figure may also hold this mystery: when, after so many eternities and ages, all things shall be restored through the Son of God, and the great Year of Jubilee <term>Year of Jubilee: Originally a biblical year of release for debts and slaves (Leviticus 25), it is used here by theosophists to describe the ultimate "restoration of all things" at the end of time.</term>, the wonder of all wonders, shall come to pass, when everything is again united with God. Then, angels and humans alike—who had divided themselves from God into so many separate states <note>original: "Schiedlichkeiten." This term refers to the fragmented, individual existence of creatures that have strayed from the primal divine unity.</note> and found no rest anywhere—will have entered into rest in God once more, until they have returned into their Center <term>Center: In German theosophy (Centrum), this refers to the divine heart or the point of origin from which all life flows and to which it must return to find peace.</term>, and God is all in all. This may well be seen in this work, as if in a mirror of the three worlds. <note>The "three worlds" usually refer to the terrestrial, celestial/paradisiacal, and divine/angelic realms discussed on the previous page.</note>

May the great God, Creator and Sustainer of Heaven and Earth, grant that we—within His light, as in the illuminated glow of grace of the Holy Spirit—may see Him as the true, perfect light, and may walk here as children of light in the light, so that we may be and remain eternally united with His light there! May God the Father, Son, and Holy Spirit help us to fulfill this; may He be highly lauded and praised for all eternity! Amen.
